<tr><td>↳反 boldness</td><td>/ˈboʊldnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大胆，勇敢</td><td>act with boldness<br>大胆行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her boldness in questioning the director impressed the whole team.  她敢于质疑总监的胆量令全组佩服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bravery</td><td>/ˈbreɪvəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，英勇</td><td>an act of bravery<br>勇敢之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was given a medal for bravery during the flood.  她因在洪水中的勇敢而获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 dauntlessness</del></td><td>/ˈdɔːntləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无畏，大胆</td><td>the dauntlessness of youth<br>年轻人的无畏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dauntlessness in the storm saved the whole crew.  他在风暴中的无畏救了全体船员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrepidity</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈpɪdəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，无畏</td><td>show great intrepidity<br>表现出极大勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot's intrepidity saved everyone aboard that night.  那晚飞行员的无畏救了机上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>trespass</b></td><td>/ˈtrespəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>擅自进入；侵入；侵犯</td><td>trespass on private land<br>非法侵入私人土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hikers who trespass on the ranch will be fined by the county.  擅自进入这座牧场的徒步者会被县里罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transgress</td><td>/trænzˈɡres/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>违背；犯错</td><td>transgress the rules<br>违反规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Any player who transgresses the rules will be removed from the tournament.  任何违反规则的选手都将被逐出这场比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invade</td><td>/ɪnˈveɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>侵入，侵犯</td><td>invade someone's privacy<br>侵犯某人隐私</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters invaded her privacy for months after the trial.  审判后记者数月侵犯她的隐私。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrude</td><td>/ɪnˈtruːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>侵入，打扰</td><td>intrude on her privacy<br>侵犯她的隐私</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope I am not intruding on your quiet evening.  希望我没有打扰你安静的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 withdraw</td><td>/wɪðˈdrɔː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>取出（款项）；撤回，撤退</td><td>withdraw money from the bank<br>从银行取钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stopped at the bank to withdraw money before the long trip.  他在长途旅行前去银行取钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retreat</td><td>/riˈtriːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>避难所，隐居处</td><td>a mountain retreat<br>山间隐居处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin became his retreat from the noise of the city.  那间小屋成了他躲避城市喧闹的避难所。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tribute</b></td><td>/ˈtrɪbjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>称颂，颂词</td><td>pay tribute to her work<br>对她的工作表示称颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues paid warm tribute to her forty years of patient teaching.  同事们对她四十年的耐心教学致以热情的颂扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accolade</td><td>/ˈækəleɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>赞扬，荣誉；奖励</td><td>win the highest accolade<br>赢得最高荣誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel received accolades from critics on both continents.  这部小说获得了两大洲评论家的赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encomium</td><td>/enˈkoʊmiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词</td><td>deliver a warm encomium<br>致以热情的赞颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor delivered a warm encomium at the retiring teacher's farewell dinner.  市长在退休教师的告别晚宴上致以热情的赞颂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 panegyric</td><td>/ˌpænəˈdʒɪrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词，颂文</td><td>deliver a long panegyric<br>发表一篇长颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retirement dinner ended with a long panegyric to the old professor.  退休晚宴以对这位老教授的长篇颂词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 denunciation</td><td>/dɪˌnʌnsiˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，公开指责</td><td>a public denunciation<br>公开的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech was a fierce denunciation of the new policy.  她的演讲是对新政策的猛烈谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 condemnation</td><td>/ˌkɑːndemˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，指责</td><td>widespread condemnation<br>广泛的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The raid drew swift condemnation from neighboring states.  这次袭击迅速招致邻国的谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 criticism</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪˌsɪzəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>批评，指责；评论</td><td>harsh criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan drew harsh criticism from residents at the meeting.  该计划在会上受到居民的严厉批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 censure</td><td>/ˈsenʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>公开表示反对，谴责</td><td>a vote of censure<br>谴责性投票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council voted to censure the mayor for his conduct.  议会投票谴责市长的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reproof</td><td>/rɪˈpruːf/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>a gentle reproof<br>温和的责备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single glance from her served as a silent reproof.  她的一个眼神就成了无声的责备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reprobation</td><td>/ˌreprəˈbeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，非难</td><td>public reprobation<br>公众谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His conduct drew strong reprobation from the medical board.  他的行为受到医学委员会的严厉谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>trickle</b></td><td>/ˈtrɪkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>一滴一滴地流，缓缓地流</td><td>trickle down the wall<br>顺墙缓缓流下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rainwater trickled down the cracked wall all through the quiet night.  雨水整夜顺着开裂的墙壁缓缓流下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pour</td><td>/ˈpɔːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>倾泻，涌流</td><td>rain pours down<br>大雨倾泻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water poured through the broken roof all afternoon.  整个下午水从破屋顶倾泻而下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 roll</td><td>/ˈroʊl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>滚滚流动，翻滚</td><td>the river rolls on<br>河水滚滚向前</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The muddy river rolled past the ruined mill without stopping.  浑浊的河水滚滚流过废弃的磨坊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stream</td><td>/ˈstriːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>流出，涌流</td><td>tears streamed down<br>泪水涌流而下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sunlight streamed through the tall windows onto the wooden floor.  阳光从高窗涌入，洒在木地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gush</td><td>/ɡʌʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>喷涌而出；滔滔不绝地说</td><td>gush from a broken pipe<br>从破裂的水管喷涌而出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water gushed from the broken pipe and flooded the basement.  水从破裂的水管喷涌而出，淹了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>trite</b></td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bathetic</del></td><td>/bəˈθetɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故作伤感的；突降的</td><td>a bathetic ending<br>故作伤感的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel ends with a bathetic scene of sudden tears.  小说以一个突然落泪、故作伤感的场面收尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threadbare</td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 timeworn</td><td>/ˈtaɪmˌwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧的，老套的</td><td>a timeworn saying<br>老掉牙的谚语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He comforted us with timeworn advice about patience and hard work.  他用关于耐心与勤奋的老套建议安慰我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fresh</td><td>/ˈfreʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新颖的；新鲜的</td><td>a fresh approach<br>新颖的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team needs a fresh approach to this old problem.  团队需要用新颖的方法处理这个老问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 new</td><td>/ˈnuː/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新的，最近出现的</td><td>a brand new car<br>一辆全新的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved into a new apartment near the river.  他们搬进了河边一套新公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 novel</td><td>/ˈnɑːvl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新奇的</td><td>a novel approach<br>新奇的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her novel approach to the old problem surprised even the senior researchers.  她对这个老问题的新奇方法连资深研究员都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unclichéd</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkliːˈʃeɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不陈腐的，新鲜的</td><td>an unclichéd ending<br>不落俗套的结尾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the film for its unclichéd dialogue.  评论家称赞这部电影不落俗套的对话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unhackneyed</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhæknid/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不陈腐的，新颖的</td><td>an unhackneyed phrase<br>一个新颖的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essay pleased the judges with its fresh, unhackneyed images.  她的文章以新鲜不俗的意象取悦了评委。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>trivial</b></td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minor</td><td>/ˈmaɪnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>较小的，不重要的</td><td>a minor problem<br>一个小问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only minor repairs were needed after the small accident.  小事故后只需要一些小修理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petty</td><td>/ˈpeti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>petty complaints<br>琐碎的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted the meeting on petty complaints about parking spaces.  他把会议浪费在关于停车位的琐碎抱怨上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weighty</td><td>/ˈweɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的；沉重的</td><td>weighty matters<br>重大的事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The elders debated weighty questions of land and water.  长者们讨论着土地与水源的重大问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indispensable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈspensəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可缺少的，必需的</td><td>an indispensable tool<br>一件不可缺少的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good dictionary is indispensable to any serious translator.  一本好词典对任何认真的译者都不可缺少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantive</td><td>/ˈsʌbstəntɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>本质的，关键的</td><td>substantive changes<br>实质性的改变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee made substantive changes to the draft rather than cosmetic ones.  委员会对草案作了实质性改动，而非表面修饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grandiose</td><td>/ˈɡrændioʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自命不凡的，浮夸的</td><td>grandiose plans<br>浮夸的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His grandiose plans for the company never survived contact with reality.  他为公司制定的浮夸计划一遇现实便破灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 massive</td><td>/ˈmæsɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，厚重的</td><td>a massive iron gate<br>厚重的铁门</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A massive wave struck the harbor wall just after midnight.  午夜刚过，一个巨浪撞上了防波堤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>truant</b></td><td>/ˈtruːənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>逃学的；逃学者，逃避责任者</td><td>play truant from school<br>逃学</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two truant boys were found fishing by the canal at noon.  两个逃学的男孩中午在运河边被发现钓鱼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shirk</td><td>/ʃɜːrk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>shirk responsibility<br>逃避责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one on this team is allowed to shirk hard work.  这个团队里没人可以逃避苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 malinger</del></td><td>/məˈlɪŋɡər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>装病以逃避工作</td><td>malinger to avoid duty<br>装病逃避职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several workers tried to malinger during the busiest week of the harvest season.  有几名工人在收获季最忙的一周里试图装病逃工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 goldbrick</del></td><td>/ˈɡoʊldbrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避工作和责任</td><td>goldbrick on the job<br>上班偷懒摸鱼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two workers were fired for goldbricking during the busiest week of the year.  两名工人因在全年最忙的一周偷懒躲活而被解雇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avoid</td><td>/əˈvɔɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，回避</td><td>avoid the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He carefully avoided answering the reporter's most difficult question.  他小心地避开了记者最难的那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 escape</td><td>/ɪˈskeɪp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>逃脱，逃避</td><td>escape punishment<br>逃脱惩罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two prisoners escaped through a tunnel under the wall.  两名囚犯从墙下的隧道逃脱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evade</td><td>/ɪˈveɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>evade the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness evaded every question about the missing money.  证人回避了所有关于失踪钱款的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parry</td><td>/ˈpæri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲避，回避（问题）</td><td>parry the question<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator tried to parry every question about his campaign funds.  那位参议员试图回避每一个关于竞选资金的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sidestep</td><td>/ˈsaɪdstep/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>回避，避开（问题）</td><td>sidestep the issue<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate neatly sidestepped the issue of rising unemployment.  这位候选人巧妙地回避了失业率上升的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circumvent</td><td>/ˌsɜːrkəmˈvent/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲避，不遵从</td><td>circumvent the rules<br>规避规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some firms hire consultants to circumvent the new tax rules.  一些公司聘请顾问来规避新的税收规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fence</td><td>/ˈfens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>栅栏，篱笆；围起</td><td>a wooden fence<br>木栅栏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They painted the wooden fence around the garden last summer.  去年夏天他们给花园周围的木栅栏刷了漆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hedge</td><td>/hedʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>树篱，篱笆；用篱笆围住</td><td>a neatly trimmed hedge<br>修剪整齐的树篱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neatly trimmed hedge separated the garden from the road.  一道修剪整齐的树篱把花园与马路隔开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avert</td><td>/əˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，防止；转移（目光）</td><td>avert a disaster<br>避免灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Quick action by the crew averted a serious accident.  船员的迅速行动避免了一场严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elude</td><td>/ɪˈluːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲闪，躲避；使无法理解，使困惑</td><td>elude the police<br>躲开警察</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief eluded the police for nearly six months before being caught.  小偷躲开警方近六个月才被抓住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shun</td><td>/ʃʌn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避开，避免</td><td>shun public attention<br>避开公众关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer chose to shun public attention after winning the prize.  那位作家获奖后选择避开公众关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skirt</td><td>/skɜːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>绕行，避开</td><td>skirt the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister tried to skirt the issue with a long, vague answer.  部长试图用冗长含糊的回答回避这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodge</td><td>/dɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避</td><td>dodge the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister managed to dodge the question three times in one interview.  部长在一次采访中三次成功回避了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bilk</td><td>/ˈbɪlk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>骗取，诈骗；逃避（债务）</td><td>bilk investors of millions<br>骗取投资者数百万</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bilked elderly clients out of their retirement savings.  他骗走了年长客户的退休储蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eschew</td><td>/ɪsˈtʃuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意避开；戒绝</td><td>eschew violence<br>戒绝暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks eschew luxury and live on whatever the village offers.  僧侣们避开奢华，靠村民所给的一切生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dutiful</td><td>/ˈduːtifəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尽职的，恭敬的</td><td>a dutiful son<br>孝顺尽责的儿子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a dutiful assistant he never once missed a deadline.  作为尽职的助手，他从未错过一次期限。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>truce</b></td><td>/truːs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>休战，休战协定</td><td>call a truce<br>宣布休战</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides agreed to a truce during the harvest season.  双方同意在收获季节休战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 armistice</td><td>/ˈɑːrməstɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>停战协定，休战</td><td>sign an armistice<br>签署停战协定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The armistice was signed in a railway carriage at dawn.  停战协定在黎明时于一节火车车厢内签署。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cease-fire</td><td>/ˈsiːsfaɪər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>停火，停火协议</td><td>a fragile cease-fire<br>脆弱的停火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cease-fire held for three days before shelling resumed.  停火维持了三天，随后炮击又开始了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peace</td><td>/ˈpiːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和平，和睦；平静</td><td>live in peace<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two villages have lived in peace for over a century.  这两个村庄和睦相处已逾一个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>truculent</b></td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belligerent</td><td>/bəˈlɪdʒərənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好战的</td><td>a belligerent tone<br>好斗的口气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the question in a belligerent tone that shocked us.  他用一种令我们震惊的好斗口气回答了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bellicose</td><td>/ˈbeləˌkoʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，挑起争斗的</td><td>bellicose rhetoric<br>好战的言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general's bellicose rhetoric alarmed the neighboring countries.  这位将军的好战言辞令邻国警觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 combative</td><td>/kəmˈbætɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好争辩的</td><td>a combative tone<br>好斗的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered every question in a combative tone.  他用好斗的语气回答每一个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 militant</td><td>/ˈmɪlətənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>激进好战的；激进分子</td><td>a militant activist<br>激进的活动人士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union took a militant stance in the pay talks.  工会在薪资谈判中采取了强硬立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggressive</td><td>/əˈɡresɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的；积极进取的，强有力的</td><td>an aggressive marketing campaign<br>强势的营销活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog became aggressive whenever strangers approached the gate.  只要有陌生人靠近大门，这只狗就变得有攻击性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feisty</td><td>/ˈfaɪsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好争论的</td><td>a feisty debater<br>好争辩的辩手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The feisty debater challenged every point her opponent made.  这位好斗的辩手对对手的每个论点都提出挑战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonaggressive</del></td><td>/ˌnɑːnəˈɡresɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好斗的，温和的</td><td>a nonaggressive approach<br>不具攻击性的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog is large but entirely nonaggressive with children.  这条狗很大，但对孩子完全不具攻击性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonbelligerent</del></td><td>/ˌnɑːnbəˈlɪdʒərənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>非交战的，不好战的</td><td>a nonbelligerent nation<br>非交战国</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty guaranteed the island's nonbelligerent status during the whole conflict.  条约保证了该岛在整场冲突期间的非交战国地位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacific</td><td>/pəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平和的，爱好和平的</td><td>a pacific temperament<br>平和的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He settled the quarrel with a few pacific words.  他用几句平和的话平息了争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peaceable</td><td>/ˈpiːsəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱好和平的；平和的</td><td>a peaceable neighbor<br>平和的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaceable villagers settled disputes without ever calling police.  这些平和的村民解决纠纷从不报警。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peaceful</td><td>/ˈpiːsfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和平的，平静的</td><td>a peaceful settlement<br>和平的解决方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally chose a peaceful path out of conflict.  双方最终选择了走出冲突的和平道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncombative</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkəmˈbætɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好斗的，不爱争的</td><td>an uncombative reply<br>不带火气的回复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her uncombative answer surprised the hostile interviewer and calmed the room.  她不带火气的回答让不友好的采访者意外，也让场面缓和下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncontentious</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈtɛnʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不引起争议的，不好争辩的</td><td>an uncontentious proposal<br>无争议的提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee began with the uncontentious items on the agenda.  委员会从议程上无争议的项目开始。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>trudge</b></td><td>/trʌdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>吃力而笨拙地走</td><td>trudge through the snow<br>在雪中吃力行走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We trudged home through deep snow with our heavy canvas bags.  我们背着沉重的帆布包，在深雪中吃力地走回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plod</td><td>/ˈplɑːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>沉重缓慢地走，苦干</td><td>plod through the mud<br>在泥地里沉重跋涉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tired horses plodded through the deep mud.  疲惫的马匹在深泥中沉重跋涉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lug</td><td>/lʌɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>费力搬运</td><td>lug a heavy suitcase<br>费力拖沉重的箱子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She lugged a heavy suitcase up four flights of narrow stairs.  她把一只沉重的箱子费力地拖上四层窄楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slog</td><td>/ˈslɑːɡ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>跋涉，艰难行进</td><td>slog through the mud<br>在泥里跋涉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We slogged through wet fields for two hours to reach the farm.  我们在湿田里跋涉两小时才到农场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flounder</td><td>/ˈflaʊndər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>笨拙地行动，挣扎</td><td>flounder in the water<br>在水中挣扎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swimmer floundered in the cold water before help arrived.  救援到来之前，游泳者在冷水中挣扎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 breeze</td><td>/ˈbriːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>轻而易举的事；微风</td><td>a breeze to finish<br>轻松就能做完</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final exam was a breeze compared with the weekly quizzes.  与每周的小测相比，期末考试轻松得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glide</td><td>/ɡlaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>轻松地行动，轻松地通过</td><td>glide across the floor<br>轻盈地滑过地板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The skaters glided across the frozen pond without making a single sound.  滑冰者们悄无声息地轻盈滑过结冰的池塘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slide</td><td>/ˈslaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>滑行，顺畅移动；下滑</td><td>slide into the room<br>悄然滑进房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She slid into the room without making any sound.  她悄无声息地滑进了房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 waltz</td><td>/ˈwɔːlts/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>轻快地走，从容前行</td><td>waltz into the room<br>轻快地走进房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waltzed into the meeting late without any apology.  他迟到了还大摇大摆地走进会场，毫无歉意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whisk</td><td>/ˈwɪsk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>迅速移动，飞快带走</td><td>whisk him away<br>飞快把他带走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guards whisked the singer away before the crowd noticed.  在人群察觉前保镖飞快把歌手带走了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flit</td><td>/flɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>轻快地飞过；掠过</td><td>flit from place to place<br>从一处快速掠到另一处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small birds flit from branch to branch searching for hidden insects.  小鸟在枝间轻快地飞来飞去寻找隐藏的昆虫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>trumpet</b></td><td>/ˈtrʌmpɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>大声说出或宣告</td><td>trumpet their success<br>大肆宣扬他们的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company trumpeted its record profits in every newspaper that week.  那一周公司在各家报纸上大肆宣扬其创纪录的利润。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advertise</td><td>/ˈædvərˌtaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>宣传，为…做广告</td><td>advertise a new brand<br>宣传新品牌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They advertised the concert on every bus in the city.  他们在全城每辆公交上宣传这场音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 annunciate</del></td><td>/əˈnʌnsieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣布，宣告</td><td>annunciate the verdict<br>宣布判决结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trumpets annunciated the arrival of the queen at the palace gate.  号角宣告女王已抵达宫门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blare</td><td>/ˈbler/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>响亮刺耳的声音；轰响</td><td>the blare of trumpets<br>响亮刺耳的号声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music blared from the open windows of the passing car.  音乐从驶过汽车敞开的车窗里轰响出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broadcast</td><td>/ˈbrɔːdˌkæst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>广播，播送；散布</td><td>broadcast the news<br>播报消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The station broadcast the news of the rescue every hour.  电台每小时播报一次营救消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 declare</td><td>/dɪˈkler/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>宣布，声明，断言</td><td>declare one's intentions<br>表明自己的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge declared the earlier verdict invalid and ordered a retrial.  法官宣布先前判决无效并下令重审。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proclaim</td><td>/prəˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣告，使…公之于众</td><td>proclaim independence<br>宣布独立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The colony voted to proclaim independence from the empire that summer.  那年夏天，殖民地投票宣告脱离帝国独立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 promulgate</td><td>/ˈprɑːməlɡeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>正式宣布</td><td>promulgate a new law<br>颁布新法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government will promulgate a new law on data privacy next month.  政府将于下月正式颁布一项新的数据隐私法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 give out</td><td>/ˌɡɪv ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发出，散发；分发</td><td>give out heat<br>散发热量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old radiator still gives out a surprising amount of heat.  这台旧暖气片散发的热量仍出人意料地多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>truncate</b></td><td>/ˈtrʌŋkeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>截短，缩短</td><td>truncate a long sentence<br>截短长句</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor truncated the article to fit the page.  编辑把文章截短以适应版面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abbreviate</td><td>/əˈbriːvieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缩写，缩短</td><td>abbreviate a long title<br>缩写长标题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You may abbreviate 'Doctor' to 'Dr.' on the envelope.  信封上可以把 Doctor 缩写为 Dr.。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abridge</td><td>/əˈbrɪdʒ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>缩短，删节</td><td>an abridged edition<br>删节版</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The publisher abridged the long novel for younger readers.  出版商为年轻读者删节了这部长篇小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curtail</td><td>/kɜːrˈteɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缩短，削减</td><td>curtail public spending<br>削减公共开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bad weather curtailed our trip by three days.  坏天气使我们的旅程缩短了三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retrench</td><td>/rɪˈtrentʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>削减开支</td><td>retrench during a recession<br>在衰退期削减开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 When orders fell, the firm decided to retrench rather than borrow more money.  订单下降时，公司决定削减开支而不再借钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elongate</td><td>/ɪˈlɔːŋɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>拉长，延长</td><td>elongate the shape<br>拉长形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tall mirrors elongate the room and make it look larger.  高镜子拉长了房间，使它显得更大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extend</td><td>/ɪkˈstend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>延长，扩展</td><td>extend a deadline<br>延长截止期限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They agreed to extend the deadline by two weeks.  他们同意把截止日期延长两周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lengthen</td><td>/ˈleŋθən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>变长，加长</td><td>lengthen a skirt<br>把裙子放长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The days lengthen noticeably after the spring equinox.  春分之后白天明显变长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prolong</td><td>/prəˈlɔːŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>延长，拖延</td><td>prolong someone's life<br>延长某人寿命</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New treatments can prolong life by several years.  新疗法能把生命延长数年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 protract</del></td><td>/prəˈtrækt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>延长，拖长</td><td>protract the negotiations<br>拖长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless objections protracted the meeting into the evening.  无休止的反对把会议拖到了晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tremendous</b></td><td>/trəˈmendəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极大的</td><td>a tremendous impact<br>极大的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new policy had a tremendous effect on small farmers everywhere.  新政策对各地小农户产生了极大的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 astronomical</td><td>/ˌæstrəˈnɑːmɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极巨大的；天文的</td><td>astronomical costs<br>极其高昂的费用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The repairs came at an astronomical cost to the city.  这些维修给市政带来了天价开支。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 colossal</td><td>/kəˈlɑːsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a colossal statue<br>巨大的雕像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A colossal statue of the founder stands in the square.  广场上矗立着创始人的巨大雕像。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elephantine</td><td>/ˌelɪˈfæntiːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；笨拙的</td><td>elephantine movements<br>笨重的动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His elephantine movements on stage made the delicate dance look almost comic.  他在台上笨重的动作让这支细腻的舞蹈显得近乎滑稽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enormous</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːrməs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>an enormous amount of data<br>庞大的数据量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project required an enormous amount of money and patience.  这个项目需要巨额资金和极大耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gargantuan</td><td>/ɡɑːrˈɡæntʃuən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a gargantuan appetite<br>巨大的食量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young athlete has a gargantuan appetite after every practice.  这名年轻运动员每次训练后都有巨大的食量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gigantic</td><td>/dʒaɪˈɡæntɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a gigantic stone statue<br>巨大的石像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gigantic stone statue guards the entrance to the temple.  一座巨大的石像守卫着神庙的入口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immense</td><td>/ɪˈmens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，极大的</td><td>an immense fortune<br>巨额财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project demanded an immense amount of time and money.  这个项目需要投入巨大的时间和金钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mammoth</td><td>/ˈmæməθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>巨大的；猛犸象</td><td>a mammoth task<br>艰巨的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rebuilding the port after the storm was a mammoth undertaking.  风暴后重建港口是一项巨大的工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 massive</td><td>/ˈmæsɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，厚重的</td><td>a massive iron gate<br>厚重的铁门</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A massive wave struck the harbor wall just after midnight.  午夜刚过，一个巨浪撞上了防波堤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monumental</td><td>/ˌmɑːnjəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>意义重大的；巨大的</td><td>a monumental achievement<br>意义重大的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building the cathedral was a monumental task that took two centuries.  建造这座大教堂是耗时两个世纪的浩大工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 titanic</td><td>/taɪˈtænɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，力量强大的</td><td>a titanic struggle<br>激烈无比的斗争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two armies fought a titanic battle across the frozen river.  两支军队在冰封的河上展开了一场惊天动地的大战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vast</td><td>/ˈvæst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，广阔的</td><td>a vast desert<br>广阔的沙漠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company owns vast tracts of forest in the north.  这家公司在北方拥有大片森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diminutive</td><td>/dɪˈmɪnjətɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的</td><td>a diminutive figure<br>矮小的身影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She noticed a diminutive figure standing quietly near the entrance.  她注意到一个小小的身影静静立在入口附近。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infinitesimal</td><td>/ˌɪnfɪnɪˈtesɪməl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极微小的，无穷小的</td><td>an infinitesimal chance<br>微乎其微的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk of infection is infinitesimal if the tools are sterile.  只要器械无菌，感染风险微乎其微。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 minuscule</td><td>/ˈmɪnəskjuːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的</td><td>a minuscule amount<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A minuscule crack in the pipe caused the entire basement to flood overnight.  管道上一道极小的裂缝一夜之间让整个地下室被淹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 minute</td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pocket</td><td>/ˈpɑːkət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>袖珍的，小型的；口袋</td><td>a pocket dictionary<br>袖珍词典</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She carried a pocket edition of the poems everywhere.  她随身带着一本袖珍版诗集。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tiny</td><td>/ˈtaɪni/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微小的</td><td>a tiny fraction<br>极小的一部分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a tiny number of applicants pass the first round.  只有极少数申请者能通过第一轮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tumult</b></td><td>/ˈtuːmʌlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，暴动</td><td>the tumult of the crowd<br>人群的骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tumult in the square grew louder as more protesters arrived.  随着更多抗议者到来，广场上的骚动越来越大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorder</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，无序；失调，疾病</td><td>public disorder<br>公共秩序混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was left in complete disorder after the party.  聚会之后房间被弄得一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturbance</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrbəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱，扰乱</td><td>cause a disturbance<br>引起骚乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police were called after a disturbance broke out in the bar.  酒吧里发生骚乱后警察被叫来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commotion</td><td>/kəˈmoʊʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱</td><td>cause a commotion<br>引起一阵骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The escaped goat caused quite a commotion in the market.  逃跑的山羊在市场上引起一阵不小的骚动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convulsion</td><td>/kənˈvʌlʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱</td><td>political convulsions<br>政治动荡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The revolution threw the entire country into political convulsions for a decade.  这场革命使整个国家陷入长达十年的政治动荡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ferment</td><td>/ˈfɜːrment/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>动乱，骚动；发酵</td><td>in a state of ferment<br>处于动荡之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole country was in a state of ferment before the election.  大选前全国处于动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turmoil</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pandemonium</td><td>/ˌpændɪˈmoʊniəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，一片混乱</td><td>pandemonium broke out<br>顿时一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pandemonium broke out when the final goal was scored.  最后一个进球时全场一片沸腾混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quietude</td><td>/ˈkwaɪətud/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，安宁</td><td>rural quietude<br>乡间的宁静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She moved north seeking the quietude of a small fishing town.  她北迁，只为寻求小渔镇的宁静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiescence</td><td>/kwiˈesəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>静止，休止状态</td><td>a period of quiescence<br>一段静止期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano entered a long period of quiescence.  这座火山进入了长期的静止期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serenity</td><td>/səˈrenəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，安详</td><td>a sense of serenity<br>一种宁静感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden offers a rare serenity in the middle of the city.  这座花园在城市中心提供了难得的宁静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquility</td><td>/træŋˈkwɪləti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，淡定</td><td>an air of tranquility<br>宁静的气息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He enjoyed the tranquility of the snowy fields at dusk.  他享受黄昏时白雪覆盖的田野的静谧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>turbid</b></td><td>/ˈtɜːrbɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混浊的</td><td>turbid river water<br>混浊的河水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the storm the once clear stream turned brown and turbid.  暴风雨过后，原本清澈的溪水变得棕黄混浊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddy</td><td>/ˈmʌdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>浑浊的，不清晰的；使难以理解</td><td>muddy the argument<br>把论点搅浑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vague statistics only muddy the argument instead of settling it.  含糊的统计数据只会把论点搅浑，而不能解决争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 murky</td><td>/ˈmɜːrki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏暗的，阴暗的；晦涩不清的</td><td>a murky, windowless basement<br>昏暗无窗的地下室</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The murky basement was lit by one failing bulb.  昏暗的地下室里只有一只快坏的灯泡照明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 limpid</td><td>/ˈlɪmpɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>透明清澈的</td><td>a limpid mountain stream<br>清澈的山间溪流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The limpid water let us count stones on the bottom.  清澈的水让我们能数清水底的石头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lucid</td><td>/ˈluːsɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，易懂的</td><td>a lucid explanation<br>清晰的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor offered a lucid summary of a difficult argument.  教授对一个艰深论证做了清晰的总结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pellucid</td><td>/pəˈluːsɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰明确的，易懂的</td><td>pellucid prose<br>明白易懂的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pellucid explanation made the theory seem obvious.  他清晰的讲解让这个理论显得浅显易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 crystalline</td><td>/ˈkrɪstəˌlaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清澈透明的；结晶的</td><td>crystalline water<br>清澈的水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crystalline lake showed every stone on its bed.  清澈的湖水把湖底每块石头都显露出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>turbulent</b></td><td>/ˈtɜːrbjələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动荡的</td><td>a turbulent period<br>动荡的时期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They married during a turbulent period of war and economic collapse.  他们在战争与经济崩溃的动荡时期结了婚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cataclysmal</del></td><td>/ˌkætəˈklɪzməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灾难性的</td><td>cataclysmal consequences<br>灾难性的后果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dam failure had cataclysmal consequences for the valley below.  大坝溃决给下游河谷带来灾难性的后果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stormy</td><td>/ˈstɔːrmi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>暴风雨的；激烈的</td><td>a stormy meeting<br>激烈的会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stormy meeting lasted until well after midnight.  这场激烈的会议一直开到午夜之后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tempestuous</td><td>/temˈpestʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂暴的，动荡不安的</td><td>a tempestuous relationship<br>剧烈动荡的关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their tempestuous marriage ended after only two stormy years.  他们剧烈动荡的婚姻仅两年就结束了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumultuous</td><td>/tuˈmʌltʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹的；动乱的</td><td>tumultuous applause<br>雷鸣般的掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist returned to tumultuous applause from the standing crowd.  钢琴家返场时全场起立，掌声雷动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>turgid</b></td><td>/ˈtɜːrdʒɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，过分装饰的</td><td>turgid prose<br>浮夸的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report's turgid style hid a very simple argument.  这份报告浮夸的文风掩盖了一个极简单的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flatulent</td><td>/ˈflætʃələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，空洞的</td><td>flatulent rhetoric<br>浮夸的辞藻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flatulent speech impressed nobody in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲没能打动任何听众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bombastic</td><td>/bɑːmˈbæstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸大的，浮夸的</td><td>bombastic rhetoric<br>浮夸的辞令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bombastic speech impressed nobody in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲没有打动任何听众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 austere</td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unadorned</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈdɔːrnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，无装饰的</td><td>unadorned walls<br>朴素的墙面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chapel was unadorned except for one small wooden cross.  礼拜堂除一个小木十字外别无装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undecorated</td><td>/ʌnˈdekəreɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未加装饰的，素净的</td><td>an undecorated room<br>未加装饰的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They left the nursery undecorated until the baby arrived.  婴儿出生前他们让育儿室一直素净着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unembellished</del></td><td>/ˌʌnɪmˈbelɪʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不加修饰的，朴实的</td><td>unembellished facts<br>不加修饰的事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told the story in plain, unembellished language.  他用朴实无华的语言讲述了这个故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>turmoil</b></td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commotion</td><td>/kəˈmoʊʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱</td><td>cause a commotion<br>引起一阵骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The escaped goat caused quite a commotion in the market.  逃跑的山羊在市场上引起一阵不小的骚动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agitation</td><td>/ˌædʒəˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>焦虑不安，激动</td><td>in a state of agitation<br>处于焦躁不安状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paced the hallway in a state of visible agitation.  他在走廊里踱步，显得焦躁不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumult</td><td>/ˈtuːmʌlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，暴动</td><td>the tumult of the crowd<br>人群的骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tumult in the square grew louder as more protesters arrived.  随着更多抗议者到来，广场上的骚动越来越大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turbulence</td><td>/ˈtɜːrbjələns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>动荡，骚乱</td><td>political turbulence<br>政局动荡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of turbulence left the country's economy in ruins.  多年动荡使这个国家经济破败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorder</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，无序；失调，疾病</td><td>public disorder<br>公共秩序混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was left in complete disorder after the party.  聚会之后房间被弄得一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ferment</td><td>/ˈfɜːrment/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>动乱，骚动；发酵</td><td>in a state of ferment<br>处于动荡之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole country was in a state of ferment before the election.  大选前全国处于动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clamor</td><td>/ˈklæmər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，喧哗，噪声</td><td>a clamor of voices<br>一片人声喧哗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A clamor of voices rose from the crowded courtyard.  拥挤的院子里传出一片人声喧哗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ease</td><td>/ˈiːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>减轻，缓和；容易</td><td>ease the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The medicine eased her headache within minutes of the first dose.  这药在服下第一剂几分钟内就缓解了她的头痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peace</td><td>/ˈpiːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和平，和睦；平静</td><td>live in peace<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two villages have lived in peace for over a century.  这两个村庄和睦相处已逾一个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peacefulness</td><td>/ˈpiːsfəlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平静，安宁</td><td>the peacefulness of dawn<br>黎明的宁静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peacefulness of the garden calmed her at once.  花园的宁静立刻让她平静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquility</td><td>/træŋˈkwɪləti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，淡定</td><td>an air of tranquility<br>宁静的气息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He enjoyed the tranquility of the snowy fields at dusk.  他享受黄昏时白雪覆盖的田野的静谧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 silence</td><td>/ˈsaɪləns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>寂静，沉默；使沉默</td><td>break the silence<br>打破沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long silence followed his surprising announcement to the class.  他向全班宣布这惊人消息后是长久的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serenity</td><td>/səˈrenəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，安详</td><td>a sense of serenity<br>一种宁静感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden offers a rare serenity in the middle of the city.  这座花园在城市中心提供了难得的宁静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>turncoat</b></td><td>/ˈtɜːrnkoʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>叛徒</td><td>a political turncoat<br>政治上的变节者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old comrades denounced him as a turncoat after he joined the rival party.  他加入敌对政党后，老战友们谴责他是叛徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 renegade</td><td>/ˈrenɪɡeɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>背教者，叛徒</td><td>a renegade from the church<br>背教出走者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once a devoted monk, he became a renegade and denounced the church openly.  他曾是虔诚的修士，后来成了背教者并公开谴责教会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 traitor</td><td>/ˈtreɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>叛徒，卖国者</td><td>hanged as a traitor<br>作为叛徒被处死</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History records him as a traitor to his own city.  历史把他记为背叛自己城市的叛徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apostate</td><td>/əˈpɑːsteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>叛教者，背弃信念者</td><td>denounced as an apostate<br>被斥为叛教者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Former comrades denounced him as an apostate and a liar.  昔日同志斥他为叛徒和骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 recreant</del></td><td>/ˈrɛkriənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>背叛者，怯懦的叛徒</td><td>branded a recreant<br>被打上叛徒烙印</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ballad calls him a recreant who fled the battle.  这首民谣称他是逃离战场的怯懦叛徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 betrayer</td><td>/bɪˈtreɪər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>背叛者，叛徒</td><td>a betrayer of trust<br>背弃信任的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him mainly as the betrayer of his own city.  历史主要记住他是自己城市的背叛者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quisling</td><td>/ˈkwɪzlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>叛国者，通敌者</td><td>branded a quisling<br>被称作叛国者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him as a quisling who served the invaders.  历史记住他是个为侵略者效劳的叛国者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loyalist</td><td>/ˈlɔɪəlɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>忠诚者，效忠者</td><td>a party loyalist<br>党内忠诚分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a few loyalists stayed with the leader after the split.  分裂后只有少数忠诚者仍跟随领袖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 partisan</td><td>/ˈpɑːrtəzn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>党派偏见的；盲目拥护者</td><td>partisan politics<br>党派偏见的政治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate quickly sank into partisan shouting about very old grievances.  辩论很快沦为带党派偏见的叫嚷，纠缠陈年旧怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adherent</td><td>/ədˈhɪrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>追随者，拥护者</td><td>a devoted adherent<br>忠实的拥护者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The movement attracted thousands of adherents in a single year.  这场运动一年内就吸引了数千名拥护者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 supporter</td><td>/səˈpɔːrtər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>支持者，拥护者</td><td>a loyal supporter<br>忠诚的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of supporters gathered outside the stadium before kickoff.  开赛前数千名支持者聚集在体育场外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>turpitude</b></td><td>/ˈtɜːrpətuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卑鄙</td><td>moral turpitude<br>道德败坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was dismissed for acts of moral turpitude.  他因道德败坏的行为被解职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abjection</td><td>/æbˈdʒekʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卑下，堕落</td><td>moral abjection<br>道德堕落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel traces one family's slow slide into abjection.  这部小说描写一个家庭缓慢堕落的过程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 corruptness</del></td><td>/kəˈrʌptnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>腐败，堕落</td><td>the corruptness of officials<br>官员的腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters were disgusted by the corruptness of the whole council.  选民对整个议会的腐败深感厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debasement</td><td>/dəˈbeɪsmənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>贬低，降格</td><td>the debasement of language<br>语言的降格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lamented the steady debasement of public political language.  他哀叹公共政治语言的持续降格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debauchery</td><td>/dəˈbɔːtʃəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放荡，纵欲</td><td>a life of debauchery<br>放荡的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted his inheritance on years of reckless debauchery.  他把遗产浪费在多年放荡纵欲上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 decadency</del></td><td>/ˈdekədənsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落，颓废</td><td>an age of decadency<br>颓废的时代</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics saw only decadency in the court's endless festivals.  批评者在宫廷无尽的盛宴中只看到颓废。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degeneration</td><td>/dɪˌdʒenəˈreɪʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>退化，衰退</td><td>the degeneration of nerve cells<br>神经细胞退化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors monitored the gradual degeneration of the patient's eyesight.  医生监测着病人视力的逐渐衰退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degradation</td><td>/ˌdeɡrəˈdeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>退化，恶化；堕落</td><td>environmental degradation<br>环境退化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rapid environmental degradation has already destroyed much of the wetland.  快速的环境退化已破坏了大部分湿地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demoralization</td><td>/dɪˌmɔːrəlɪˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>道德败坏；士气涣散</td><td>widespread demoralization<br>普遍的道德败坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Defeat brought demoralization to every unit on the front.  战败使前线各部队士气涣散、风纪败坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depravity</td><td>/dɪˈprævəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>道德败坏</td><td>sink into utter depravity<br>堕入彻底的道德败坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trial revealed a level of depravity that shocked the small town.  庭审揭露出的道德败坏程度震惊了这个小镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dissipatedness</del></td><td>/ˈdɪsəpeɪtɪdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放荡，荒淫</td><td>youthful dissipatedness<br>年轻时的放荡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dissipatedness ruined both his health and his fortune.  他的放荡毁了他的健康和财产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dissoluteness</del></td><td>/ˈdɪsəluːtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放纵；生活淫荡</td><td>a life of dissoluteness<br>放纵的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of dissoluteness ruined both his health and his fortune.  多年的放纵毁了他的健康和财产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 libertinism</del></td><td>/ˈlɪbərtɪnɪzəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放纵，淫荡</td><td>aristocratic libertinism<br>贵族的放纵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play mocks the libertinism of the fashionable set.  该剧讽刺了时髦阶层的放纵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perversion</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrʒən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落；反常；曲解</td><td>a perversion of justice<br>对正义的曲解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such a ruling is a perversion of the law.  这样的裁决是对法律的曲解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 rakishness</del></td><td>/ˈreɪkɪʃnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放浪，浮荡</td><td>a hint of rakishness<br>一丝浮荡之气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a careless rakishness in the way he dressed.  他的穿着带着一种随意的浮荡气质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 probity</td><td>/ˈproʊbəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直</td><td>unquestioned probity<br>毋庸置疑的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge was known for probity and unfailing patience.  这位法官以正直和永不失去的耐心闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>trope</b></td><td>/troʊp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>比喻；陈词滥调</td><td>a familiar literary trope<br>常见的文学套路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lonely detective has become a familiar trope in modern crime fiction.  孤独的侦探已成为现代犯罪小说里常见的套路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 metaphor</td><td>/ˈmetəfər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>暗喻</td><td>a mixed metaphor<br>混杂的暗喻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech collapsed into a mixed metaphor about ships, mountains, and rising tides.  这篇演讲陷入了船、山与涨潮混杂的暗喻之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simile</td><td>/ˈsɪməli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>明喻</td><td>an apt simile<br>贴切的明喻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet uses an apt simile to compare memory with drifting smoke.  诗人用一个贴切的明喻把记忆比作飘散的烟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banality</td><td>/bəˈnæləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平庸；陈腐的话</td><td>the banality of his speech<br>他讲话的平庸乏味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film offered nothing but banalities about growing up.  这部电影只提供了些关于成长的陈腐说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bromide</td><td>/ˈbroʊmaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调；镇静剂</td><td>a tired old bromide<br>老掉牙的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech ended with a tired old bromide about hard work.  他的演讲以一句关于努力工作的陈腐套话作结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 platitude</td><td>/ˈplætɪtuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调</td><td>utter a platitude<br>说一句陈词滥调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech offered nothing but platitudes about hard work and dreams.  这篇演讲除了关于努力与梦想的陈词滥调外别无他物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truism</td><td>/ˈtruːɪzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调</td><td>a familiar truism<br>一句熟悉的老话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is a truism that hard work alone rarely guarantees success.  仅靠努力很少能保证成功，这是句老生常谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>typo</b></td><td>/ˈtaɪpoʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>打字错误；排印错误</td><td>correct a typo<br>改正一个错字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single typo in the headline embarrassed the whole editorial staff.  标题里的一个错字让整个编辑部很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misprint</td><td>/ˈmɪsprɪnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>印刷错误</td><td>an obvious misprint<br>明显的印刷错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The date on the ticket was a misprint, not a change of plan.  票上的日期是印错了，不是改了计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bug</td><td>/ˈbʌɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>程序错误；小虫</td><td>fix a software bug<br>修复软件缺陷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tiny bug in the code crashed the entire system.  代码里一个小错误让整个系统崩溃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tyro</b></td><td>/ˈtaɪroʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，业余爱好者</td><td>a tyro at chess<br>国际象棋新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a tyro at chess, he lost every game that afternoon.  作为国际象棋新手，他那天下午每局都输了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 novice</td><td>/ˈnɑːvɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，初学者</td><td>a complete novice<br>彻底的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A complete novice, he had never held a chisel before that afternoon.  他是个彻底的新手，那天下午之前从没握过凿子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apprentice</td><td>/əˈprentəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>学徒，新手</td><td>a carpenter's apprentice<br>木匠学徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He started as an apprentice and now owns the shop.  他从学徒做起，现在拥有这家店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neophyte</td><td>/ˈniːəfaɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>初学者，新手</td><td>a neophyte in politics<br>政界的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a neophyte in politics, he underestimated how slowly legislation actually moves.  作为政界的新手，他低估了立法进程实际有多缓慢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rookie</td><td>/ˈrʊki/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新兵；新手；菜鸟</td><td>a rookie mistake<br>新手才犯的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rookie made a nervous throw that cost his team the game.  那名新手一次紧张的传球让球队输掉了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amateur</td><td>/ˈæməˌtɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>业余爱好者；外行的</td><td>a talented amateur<br>有天赋的业余者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comet was first spotted by an amateur astronomer.  这颗彗星最先由一位业余天文学家发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dabbler</td><td>/ˈdæblər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>浅尝辄止者，业余涉猎者</td><td>a dabbler in poetry<br>诗歌上的业余涉猎者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained a cheerful dabbler in half a dozen crafts.  他一直是好几门手艺的快乐业余涉猎者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dilettante</td><td>/ˌdɪləˈtænti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>浅尝辄止的业余爱好者；不专业的</td><td>a mere dilettante<br>只是业余涉猎者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a mere dilettante in astronomy, he owned a telescope but read little.  作为天文学上的业余涉猎者，他有望远镜却几乎不读书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fledgling</td><td>/ˈfledʒlɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>新手</td><td>a fledgling company<br>新兴的公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fledgling airline struggled through its very first winter.  这家新兴航空公司艰难度过了第一个冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recruit</td><td>/rəˈkruːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>新成员，新兵；招募</td><td>a raw recruit<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every new recruit spends six weeks in basic training.  每名新兵都要接受六周基础训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 old hand</td><td>/oʊld ˈhænd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老手，行家</td><td>an old hand at negotiation<br>谈判老手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As an old hand at winter climbing, he read the weather easily.  作为冬季攀登老手，他轻松读懂了天气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 old-timer</td><td>/ˌoʊldˈtaɪmər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老资格的人，老前辈</td><td>ask an old-timer<br>请教老前辈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old-timers in the shop still repair watches by hand.  店里的老师傅们仍手工修表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vet</td><td>/ˈvet/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老兵，老手</td><td>a twenty-year vet<br>二十年的老手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team hired a vet of three Olympic campaigns as coach.  球队聘请了一位三届奥运老将当教练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expert</td><td>/ˈekspərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>专家；熟练的，专业的</td><td>a leading expert<br>顶尖专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called in an expert to examine the cracked foundation.  他们请来专家检查开裂的地基。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 professional</td><td>/prəˈfeʃənəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>专业的；专业人士</td><td>a professional musician<br>专业的音乐家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a professional could repair such a delicate mechanism.  只有专业人士才能修好如此精密的机构。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 veteran</td><td>/ˈvetərən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老兵；有丰富经验的人</td><td>a veteran journalist<br>资深记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A veteran journalist, she has covered four wars and six elections.  作为资深记者，她报道过四场战争和六次选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ubiquitous</b></td><td>/juːˈbɪkwɪtəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无所不在的，普遍存在的</td><td>ubiquitous smartphones<br>随处可见的手机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap sensors are now ubiquitous in cars, phones, and doorbells.  廉价传感器如今在汽车、手机和门铃里无处不在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 omnipresent</td><td>/ˌɑːmnɪˈprezənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无处不在的</td><td>omnipresent cameras<br>无处不在的摄像头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Advertising is omnipresent in the corridors of the station.  车站走廊里广告无处不在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 universal</td><td>/ˌjuːnəˈvɜːrsəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，全体的</td><td>universal human rights<br>普世人权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music seems to have a universal appeal across all cultures.  音乐似乎在所有文化中都有普遍的感染力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 general</td><td>/ˈdʒenərəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>总体的，普遍的；笼统的</td><td>in general terms<br>笼统地讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This book gives a general introduction to ancient Greek history.  这本书对古希腊史作了总体介绍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraordinary</td><td>/ɪkˈstrɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，特别的</td><td>extraordinary courage<br>非凡的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took extraordinary courage to report what she had seen.  举报她所见之事需要非凡的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infrequent</td><td>/ˌɪnˈfriːkwənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀少的，不常发生的</td><td>infrequent visits<br>难得的探访</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His visits home became increasingly infrequent.  他回家的次数越来越少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rare</td><td>/ˈrer/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀少的，罕见的</td><td>a rare bird<br>罕见的鸟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is rare to see snow here in April.  四月这里下雪很罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seldom</td><td>/ˈseldəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>副词</td><td>很少，不常</td><td>seldom if ever<br>几乎从不</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seldom answers the phone before ten in the morning.  他早上十点前很少接电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncommon</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不常见的，罕有的</td><td>not uncommon<br>并不罕见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such mistakes are not uncommon among beginners.  这类错误在初学者中并不罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfamiliar</td><td>/ˌʌnfəˈmɪljər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟悉的，陌生的</td><td>an unfamiliar face<br>一张陌生的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The street names here were all unfamiliar to me.  这里的街名对我来说都很陌生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unusual</td><td>/ʌnˈjuːʒuəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，罕见的</td><td>an unusual coincidence<br>罕见的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was an unusual coincidence that both letters arrived together.  两封信同时到达是个罕见的巧合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unique</td><td>/juˈniːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>独一无二的，独特的</td><td>a unique opportunity<br>独一无二的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each fingerprint is unique to the person who made it.  每个指纹都是其主人独有的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 particular</td><td>/pərˈtɪkjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>详细的；特定的；讲究的</td><td>a particular account of events<br>对事件的详细说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a particular account of every repair the house needed.  他详细说明了房子所需的每一项修缮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>unassailable</b></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈseɪləbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可争辩的，无法否认的，不可亵渎的</td><td>an unassailable position<br>无法攻破的地位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Decades of careful research gave her an unassailable reputation among historians.  数十年的严谨研究使她在史学界享有无可争辩的声誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undeniable</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈnaɪəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可否认的，明显的</td><td>undeniable talent<br>不可否认的才华</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her influence on modern dance is undeniable.  她对现代舞的影响不可否认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inviolable</td><td>/ˌɪnˈvaɪələbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可侵犯的，神圣的</td><td>an inviolable right<br>不可侵犯的权利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Freedom of conscience is treated here as an inviolable principle.  在这里，良心自由被视为不可侵犯的原则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sacrosanct</td><td>/ˈsækroʊsæŋkt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极为神圣的，不可侵犯的</td><td>regarded as sacrosanct<br>被视为不可侵犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In this office the lunch hour is treated as absolutely sacrosanct.  在这间办公室里，午餐时间被视为绝对不可侵犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irrefutable</td><td>/ˌɪrɪˈfjuːtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可反驳的</td><td>irrefutable evidence<br>无可反驳的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letters gave irrefutable proof of his involvement.  这些信件提供了他参与其中的铁证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indisputable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈspjuːtəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可争辩的</td><td>indisputable evidence<br>无可争辩的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photographs offered indisputable proof of the damage.  这些照片提供了损害的无可争辩的证明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unexceptionable</del></td><td>/ˌʌnɪkˈsepʃənəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可挑剔的，无可指责的</td><td>unexceptionable manners<br>无可挑剔的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her conduct throughout the inquiry was unexceptionable.  在整个调查中她的行为无可指责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unimpeachable</td><td>/ˌʌnɪmˈpiːtʃəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可怀疑的，无懈可击的</td><td>unimpeachable integrity<br>无可置疑的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness had an unimpeachable record of public service.  这位证人有无可指责的公职记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 controversial</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈvɜːrʃəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有争议的，引起争论的</td><td>a controversial decision<br>有争议的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge's controversial ruling was debated for months afterward.  法官那项有争议的裁决之后被讨论了数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>uncanny</b></td><td>/ʌnˈkæni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>离奇的，奇异的</td><td>an uncanny resemblance<br>异乎寻常的相似</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins share an uncanny ability to finish each other's sentences.  这对双胞胎有一种离奇的本事，能接上对方的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magical</td><td>/ˈmædʒɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>魔法的，神奇的</td><td>a magical atmosphere<br>神奇的氛围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Candlelight gave the small chapel a magical atmosphere.  烛光让这座小教堂有了神奇的氛围。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miraculous</td><td>/mɪˈrækjələs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇迹般的，超乎自然的</td><td>a miraculous recovery<br>奇迹般的康复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors called her recovery nothing short of miraculous.  医生称她的康复简直是奇迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phenomenal</td><td>/fəˈnɑːmɪnl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，非凡的</td><td>phenomenal success<br>非凡的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel enjoyed phenomenal success in a dozen countries.  她的第一部小说在十几个国家取得了非凡的成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preternatural</td><td>/ˌpriːtərˈnætʃrəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超乎寻常的</td><td>preternatural calm<br>异乎寻常的平静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot showed preternatural calm while the engine was clearly failing.  发动机明显故障时，飞行员表现出超乎寻常的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superhuman</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈhjuːmən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>超人的，非凡的</td><td>superhuman strength<br>超人的力量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lifting the beam alone took almost superhuman strength.  单人抬起那根梁几乎需要超人的力量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supernormal</td><td>/ˌsupərˈnɔrməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超常的，异乎寻常的</td><td>supernormal hearing<br>超常的听力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog showed supernormal sensitivity to distant thunder.  这只狗对远处的雷声表现出超常的敏感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transcendent</td><td>/trænˈsendənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卓越的，超凡的</td><td>transcendent beauty<br>超凡的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her transcendent performance silenced the entire hall.  她超凡的演出让全场安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transcendental</td><td>/ˌtrænsenˈdentəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>超验的，超自然的</td><td>a transcendental experience<br>超验的体验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He described a transcendental moment on the mountain top.  他描述了在山顶的一次超验体验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unearthly</td><td>/ʌnˈɜːrθli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非尘世的，诡异恐怖的</td><td>an unearthly silence<br>诡异的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An unearthly cry came from somewhere deep in the forest.  森林深处传来一声诡异的叫声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ordinary</td><td>/ˈɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普通的，平常的</td><td>just an ordinary weekday<br>只是平常的工作日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked like an ordinary commuter waiting for the morning bus.  他看上去就像个等早班公交的普通上班族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 normal</td><td>/ˈnɔːrməl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>正常的；常态</td><td>back to normal<br>恢复正常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everything returned to normal after the power was restored.  供电恢复后一切都回归正常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>uncouth</b></td><td>/ʌnˈkuːθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无教养的</td><td>uncouth table manners<br>粗野的餐桌举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncouth remarks at dinner offended nearly everyone at the table.  他在晚餐上粗鲁的言辞几乎惹恼了同桌所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crass</td><td>/kræs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，愚钝的</td><td>crass commercialism<br>赤裸裸的商业气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His crass joke silenced the entire table at dinner.  他粗俗的玩笑让整桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rude</td><td>/ˈruːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无礼的</td><td>a rude remark<br>无礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was rude of him to leave without thanking the host.  他不谢主人就离开，很没礼貌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discourteous</td><td>/dɪsˈkɜːrtiəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无礼的，粗鲁的</td><td>a discourteous reply<br>无礼的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was discourteous to leave without thanking the host.  不向主人道谢就离开是无礼的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disgracious</del></td><td>/dɪsˈɡreɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无礼的，不客气的</td><td>a disgracious reply<br>无礼的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His disgracious tone offended even his closest allies.  他无礼的语气连最亲近的盟友都得罪了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ungraceful</del></td><td>/ʌnˈɡreɪsfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不优雅的，笨拙的</td><td>an ungraceful landing<br>不优雅的落地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's ungraceful stumble was noticed by the judges.  舞者笨拙的踉跄被评委看到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crude</td><td>/ˈkruːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，未加工的；粗俗的</td><td>crude oil<br>原油</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The furniture was crude but surprisingly sturdy and very cheap.  这家具做得粗糙，却意外结实又便宜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ill-bred</td><td>/ˌɪl ˈbred/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没教养的，粗野的</td><td>ill-bred behavior<br>没教养的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was ill-bred of him to leave without thanking the host.  他不谢主人就离开，实在没教养。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ill-mannered</td><td>/ˌɪl ˈmænərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>举止粗鲁的，没礼貌的</td><td>an ill-mannered guest<br>没礼貌的客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ill-mannered boy grabbed food before the guests sat down.  这个没礼貌的男孩在客人坐下前就抓起食物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
