<tr><td colspan="7">例 Countless letters arrived after the interview was broadcast.  访谈播出后寄来了无数信件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 innumerous</del></td><td>/ɪˈnumərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无数的，数不尽的</td><td>innumerous stars<br>无数星辰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Innumerous insects filled the air above the marsh at dusk.  黄昏时沼泽上空满是数不尽的昆虫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncountable</td><td>/ʌnˈkaʊntəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>数不清的，不可数的</td><td>uncountable grains of sand<br>数不清的沙粒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The beach holds uncountable grains of pale sand.  这片海滩有数不清的浅色沙粒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncounted</td><td>/ˌʌnˈkaʊntɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未计数的，无数的</td><td>uncounted victims<br>未被计入的受害者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Uncounted villages disappeared from the maps after the war.  战后有无数村庄从地图上消失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 countable</td><td>/ˈkaʊntəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可数的，可计算的</td><td>countable losses<br>可以计数的损失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The damage was small and entirely countable in a single page.  损失不大，一页纸就能全部列清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enumerable</td><td>/ɪˈnumərəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可列举的，可计数的</td><td>enumerable options<br>可以逐一列举的选项</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee faced only three enumerable choices that morning.  那天上午委员会只面对三个可逐一列举的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 numberable</del></td><td>/ˈnʌmbərəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可计数的</td><td>numberable items<br>可计数的物品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everything in the small storeroom was numberable within an hour.  小储藏室里的一切一小时内就能数清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quantifiable</td><td>/ˌkwɑːntɪˈfaɪəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可量化的</td><td>quantifiable benefits<br>可量化的收益</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme produced no quantifiable benefits for local families.  该方案没有给当地家庭带来可量化的收益。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calculable</td><td>/ˈkælkjələbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可计算的，可估量的</td><td>calculable risk<br>可估量的风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers prefer a calculable risk to an unknown one.  工程师宁要可估量的风险，也不要未知风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>untoward</b></td><td>/ʌnˈtɔːrd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不幸的；难以驯服的，难以驾驭的</td><td>an untoward incident<br>意外的不幸事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing untoward happened during the long flight across the ocean.  在这趟跨洋长途飞行中没有发生任何不幸的意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unpropitious</del></td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈpɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不吉利的，不利的</td><td>an unpropitious start<br>不利的开端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project began under distinctly unpropitious circumstances.  该项目在明显不利的情况下启动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unlucky</td><td>/ʌnˈlʌki/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不幸的，倒霉的</td><td>an unlucky guess<br>不走运的猜测</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was unlucky to miss the last train by seconds.  他很倒霉，差几秒钟没赶上末班车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misfortunate</del></td><td>/mɪsˈfɔrtʃənət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不幸的，倒霉的</td><td>a misfortunate traveler<br>倒霉的旅人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One misfortunate passenger missed both connecting trains.  一位倒霉的乘客错过了两趟接续列车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfortunate</td><td>/ʌnˈfɔːrtʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不幸的，遗憾的</td><td>an unfortunate mistake<br>不幸的失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was unfortunate that the letter arrived a week too late.  遗憾的是那封信迟到了一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 froward</td><td>/ˈfroʊərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倔强的，不服从的</td><td>a froward apprentice<br>倔强的学徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The froward apprentice ignored every warning from his master.  这倔强的学徒无视师父的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 headstrong</td><td>/ˈhedˌstrɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>任性的，固执的</td><td>a headstrong child<br>任性的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headstrong young man ignored every warning from his parents.  这个任性的年轻人对父母的每句劝告都置之不理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intractable</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtræktəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难对付的，棘手的</td><td>an intractable problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poverty remains an intractable problem in many rural regions.  贫困在许多农村地区仍是棘手的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recalcitrant</td><td>/rɪˈkælsɪtrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽抗的，不顺从的</td><td>a recalcitrant employee<br>不听劝的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A recalcitrant employee refused every instruction his new supervisor gave him.  一名顽抗的员工拒绝了新主管给他的每一条指示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refractory</td><td>/rɪˈfræktəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倔强的，不顺从的</td><td>a refractory pupil<br>不顺从的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One refractory pupil disrupted the lesson for the entire class.  一个不顺从的学生扰乱了全班的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unruly</td><td>/ʌnˈruːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难驾驭的，不守规矩的</td><td>an unruly crowd<br>不守规矩的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two teachers struggled to calm the unruly class.  两位老师费力地安抚那个不守规矩的班级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wayward</td><td>/ˈweɪwərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>任性的，不服管束的</td><td>a wayward son<br>不服管束的儿子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their wayward son ignored every rule his patient parents set.  他们不服管束的儿子无视父母耐心定下的每条规矩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 willful</td><td>/ˈwɪlfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，倔强的</td><td>willful disobedience<br>故意的违抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His willful refusal to ask for help delayed the repair by weeks.  他固执地拒绝求助，使维修推迟了好几周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 auspicious</td><td>/ɔːˈspɪʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好兆头的</td><td>an auspicious beginning<br>吉利的开端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain on the wedding day was considered an auspicious beginning.  婚礼当天下雨被认为是个好兆头的开端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propitious</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吉祥的</td><td>a propitious moment<br>吉利的时机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They waited for a propitious moment to announce the merger.  他们等待一个吉利的时机来宣布合并。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 favorable</td><td>/ˈfeɪvərəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的</td><td>favorable conditions<br>有利的条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Favorable weather allowed the harvest to finish a week early.  有利的天气让收割提前一周完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fortunate</td><td>/ˈfɔːrtʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>幸运的</td><td>a fortunate escape<br>幸运的脱险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We were fortunate that the storm turned east at the last hour.  我们很幸运，风暴最后一小时转向东去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 controllable</td><td>/kənˈtroʊləbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可控制的</td><td>controllable costs<br>可控成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fuel use is one of the few controllable costs on the farm.  燃油是农场少数可控成本之一。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 governable</del></td><td>/ˈɡʌvərnəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可管理的，易统治的</td><td>a governable class<br>一个好管的班级</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With patience the once wild horse became perfectly governable.  在耐心训练下，这匹曾野性难驯的马变得十分听话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 manageable</td><td>/ˈmænɪdʒəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易管理的，好对付的</td><td>a manageable workload<br>可应付的工作量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Split the task into smaller, manageable pieces first.  先把任务拆成较小、好处理的部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tractable</td><td>/ˈtræktəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易驾驭的，温顺的</td><td>a tractable student<br>听话的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppy proved surprisingly tractable during the first weeks of training.  这只小狗在最初几周的训练中出乎意料地听话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>unwitting</b></td><td>/ʌnˈwɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知道的，未觉察的</td><td>an unwitting accomplice<br>不知情的帮凶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became an unwitting participant in the whole scheme.  她不知不觉地成了整个阴谋的参与者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unaware</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈwer/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知道的，未察觉的</td><td>be unaware of the rule<br>不知道这条规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They were unaware that the meeting had been moved to Friday.  他们不知道会议已改到周五。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignorant</td><td>/ˈɪɡnərənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知的，不知道的</td><td>ignorant of the facts<br>不了解事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They were completely ignorant of the danger ahead.  他们对前方的危险毫不知情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acquainted</td><td>/əˈkweɪntɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，认识的</td><td>be acquainted with<br>熟悉；与……相识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I am not well acquainted with the rules of this game.  我对这个游戏的规则不太熟悉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aware</td><td>/əˈwer/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>知道的，意识到的</td><td>be aware of the danger<br>意识到危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was fully aware that the deadline had already passed.  她完全知道截止日期已经过了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cognizant</td><td>/ˈkɑːɡnɪzənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>知道的，意识到的</td><td>be cognizant of risks<br>意识到风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors must be cognizant of the risks before signing anything.  投资者在签署任何文件前必须意识到风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conscious</td><td>/ˈkɑːnʃəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有意识的，察觉到的</td><td>a conscious decision<br>有意识的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a conscious effort to speak more slowly on stage.  他有意识地努力在台上讲得慢一些。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conversant</td><td>/kənˈvɜːrsnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，通晓的</td><td>conversant with the law<br>通晓法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is fully conversant with the new safety regulations.  她非常熟悉新的安全条例。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grounded</td><td>/ˈɡraʊndɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>在地面上的，停飞的</td><td>grounded by the storm<br>因暴风雨而停飞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All flights were grounded because of the heavy fog.  由于大雾，所有航班都被迫停飞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 informed</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrmd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>见识广的，掌握信息的</td><td>make an informed decision<br>做出明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters need accurate data to make an informed choice.  选民需要准确的数据才能做出明智的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 knowing</td><td>/ˈnoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故意的，明知的</td><td>a knowing violation of the rules<br>明知故犯的违规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge called it a knowing violation of the safety rules.  法官称这是明知故犯地违反安全规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mindful</td><td>/ˈmaɪndfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>mindful of the risks<br>留意风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be mindful of the time when you answer each question.  回答每个问题时要留意时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>unwonted</b></del></td><td>/ʌnˈwoʊntɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，不习惯的</td><td>unwonted silence<br>异乎寻常的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An unwonted silence fell over the noisy classroom.  嘈杂的教室里出现了不寻常的安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncommon</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不常见的，罕有的</td><td>not uncommon<br>并不罕见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such mistakes are not uncommon among beginners.  这类错误在初学者中并不罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unordinary</del></td><td>/ʌnˈɔrdəneri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，罕见的</td><td>an unordinary request<br>不寻常的请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Snow in October is quite unordinary in this valley.  在这个山谷，十月下雪相当不寻常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unusual</td><td>/ʌnˈjuːʒuəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，罕见的</td><td>an unusual coincidence<br>罕见的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was an unusual coincidence that both letters arrived together.  两封信同时到达是个罕见的巧合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wonted</td><td>/ˈwoʊntɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>惯常的，习惯的</td><td>his wonted seat<br>他惯常的座位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man took his wonted place beside the stove.  老人坐到炉边他惯常的位子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 usual</td><td>/ˈjuːʒuəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>通常的，惯常的</td><td>the usual routine<br>惯常的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at his usual time and took his usual seat.  他按老时间到达，坐在老位子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 routine</td><td>/ruˈtiːn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>例行的；常规程序</td><td>a routine check<br>例行检查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The problem was discovered during a routine inspection last week.  这个问题是上周例行检查时发现的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 habitual</td><td>/həˈbɪtʃuəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>习惯性的，惯常的</td><td>his habitual morning walk<br>他惯常的晨间散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Habitual lateness eventually cost him the job.  惯常迟到最终让他失去了工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accustomed</td><td>/əˈkʌstəmd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>习惯的，惯常的</td><td>her accustomed routine<br>她惯常的作息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He soon grew accustomed to working through the night.  他很快习惯了通夜工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 customary</td><td>/ˈkʌstəˌmeri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>习惯的，惯常的</td><td>the customary greeting<br>惯常的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is customary here to remove your shoes at the door.  这里习惯在门口脱鞋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>upbraid</b></del></td><td>/ʌpˈbreɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（严厉地）谴责，责骂</td><td>upbraid him for lying<br>因撒谎而责骂他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager upbraided him for missing three deadlines in one month.  经理因他一个月内三次误期而严厉责骂他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baste</td><td>/ˈbeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛打；严厉批评</td><td>baste him with criticism<br>用批评痛击他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist basted the mayor for wasting so much public money.  专栏作家痛批市长浪费了大量公款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scold</td><td>/ˈskoʊld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>责骂，训斥</td><td>scold the children<br>责骂孩子们</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher scolded the boys for running through the hallway.  老师因男孩们在走廊跑动而训斥他们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revile</td><td>/riˈvaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>辱骂，斥责</td><td>revile his opponents<br>辱骂对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Newspapers reviled the mayor for weeks after the scandal broke.  丑闻曝光后报纸辱骂市长数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 berate</td><td>/bɪˈreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉指责，斥责</td><td>berate him publicly<br>公开斥责他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach began to berate his players in front of reporters.  教练开始在记者面前严厉斥责他的球员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 vituperate</del></td><td>/vaɪˈtuːpəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谩骂，责骂</td><td>vituperate an opponent<br>谩骂对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist vituperated the mayor in every weekly article.  这位专栏作家在每周的文章里都痛骂市长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glorify</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，美化</td><td>glorify war and violence<br>美化战争与暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the film glorified war instead of questioning it.  批评者说这部电影美化战争而非质疑它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hymn</td><td>/hɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞歌，赞美诗</td><td>sing a hymn<br>唱赞美诗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole congregation stood up to sing a hymn of thanksgiving.  全体教众站起身来唱一首感恩的赞美诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 magnify</td><td>/ˈmæɡnəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放大；夸大</td><td>magnify the problem<br>夸大问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens magnifies the specimen four hundred times.  这个镜头把样本放大四百倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 panegyrize</del></td><td>/ˈpænɪdʒəraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>赞颂，写颂词</td><td>panegyrize a fallen leader<br>赞颂逝去的领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Court poets panegyrized the emperor in long ceremonial odes.  宫廷诗人以长篇颂歌赞颂皇帝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flatter</td><td>/ˈflætər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>奉承；讨好；谄媚</td><td>flatter the boss<br>奉承老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flattered the boss all morning hoping for a promotion.  他整个上午奉承老板，希望得到升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fawn</td><td>/fɔːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>阿谀奉承</td><td>fawn on the boss<br>讨好老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Junior staff fawn on the boss whenever the director visits.  每当董事来访，下级员工就对老板阿谀奉承。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cringe</td><td>/ˈkrɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝；畏缩</td><td>cringe before the boss<br>在老板面前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The servant cringed whenever the duke raised his voice.  公爵一提高声音，仆人就畏缩起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adulterate</td><td>/əˈdʌltəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>掺杂，加入低等成分</td><td>adulterate milk with water<br>在牛奶里掺水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dealer was fined for adulterating olive oil with cheaper fat.  这个商人因在橄榄油中掺入廉价油脂而被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>uphold</b></td><td>/ʌpˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，赞成；维持（原判）</td><td>uphold a decision<br>维持原判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The appeals court chose to uphold the original ruling in full.  上诉法院决定完全维持原判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 support</td><td>/səˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，支撑</td><td>support a proposal<br>支持一项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most members voted to support the new proposal.  大多数成员投票支持这项新提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bolster</td><td>/ˈboʊlstər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，增强</td><td>bolster his confidence<br>增强他的信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A word of praise from her would bolster his confidence enormously.  她一句赞扬就会极大地增强他的信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advocate</td><td>/ˈædvəkeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，提倡</td><td>advocate reform<br>提倡改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many doctors advocate walking as the simplest form of exercise.  许多医生提倡把走路作为最简单的运动方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 champion</td><td>/ˈtʃæmpiən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持</td><td>champion a cause<br>支持一项事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has championed the rights of migrant workers for years.  她多年来一直为农民工的权益奔走呼吁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endorse</td><td>/ɪnˈdɔːrs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开支持</td><td>endorse a candidate<br>公开支持某候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union voted to endorse the mayor for a second term.  工会投票支持市长连任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 espouse</td><td>/ɪˈspaʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，拥护</td><td>espouse a cause<br>拥护一项事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She openly espoused the cause of prison reform.  她公开拥护监狱改革事业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>urbane</b></td><td>/ərˈbeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彬彬有礼的，文雅的</td><td>an urbane host<br>文雅的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our urbane host guided the conversation with easy wit and grace.  我们文雅的主人以轻松的机智和风度引导着谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debonair</td><td>/ˌdebəˈner/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>风度翩翩的，温文尔雅的</td><td>a debonair manner<br>从容优雅的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debonair gentleman charmed every woman in the crowded ballroom.  这位风度翩翩的绅士让拥挤舞厅里的每位女士都为之倾倒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 civilized</td><td>/ˈsɪvəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>文明的，有教养的</td><td>civilized behavior<br>文明的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute in a calm, civilized manner over tea.  他们喝茶时以平静文明的方式解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genteel</td><td>/dʒenˈtiːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，不粗俗的</td><td>genteel manners<br>有教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her genteel accent betrayed an expensive and very traditional upbringing.  她文雅的口音透露出昂贵而传统的成长背景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultivated</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultivated taste in art<br>高雅的艺术品位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cultivated manners impressed everyone at the dinner.  她优雅的举止让晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultured</td><td>/ˈkʌltʃərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultured gentleman<br>有教养的绅士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a cultured man who read poetry in four languages.  他是个有教养的人，能读四种语言的诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polished</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-bred</td><td>/wɛl ˈbrɛd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有礼貌的</td><td>a well-bred young woman<br>一位有教养的年轻女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The well-bred guest thanked the host before leaving.  这位有教养的客人离开前向主人道了谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 courteous</td><td>/ˈkɜːrtiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>礼貌的，谦恭有礼的</td><td>a courteous reply<br>礼貌的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even when customers shouted, the young clerk remained calm and courteous.  即使顾客大声嚷嚷，那位年轻店员仍然平静而有礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gracious</td><td>/ˈɡreɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和善的，亲切的</td><td>a gracious host<br>亲切的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a gracious host who made every guest feel welcome.  她是位亲切的主人，让每位客人都感到受欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 boorish</td><td>/ˈbʊrɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁无礼的</td><td>boorish behavior<br>粗鲁无礼的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His boorish behavior at the table embarrassed everyone present.  他在餐桌上粗鲁无礼的举动让在场所有人难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 churlish</td><td>/ˈtʃɜːrlɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁无礼的</td><td>a churlish refusal<br>无礼的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be churlish to refuse such a generous gift.  拒绝如此丰厚的礼物就太无礼了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 classless</td><td>/ˈklæsləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，没有教养的</td><td>a classless remark<br>一句粗俗的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mocking the waiter was a classless thing to do.  取笑服务员是很没教养的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clownish</td><td>/ˈklaʊnɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>小丑般的，滑稽粗俗的</td><td>clownish antics<br>滑稽的怪动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His clownish antics amused the children but embarrassed his wife.  他滑稽的怪动作逗乐了孩子，却让妻子难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loutish</td><td>/ˈlaʊtɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的</td><td>loutish manners<br>粗鲁的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His loutish manners at the dinner table embarrassed his entire family.  他在餐桌上粗鲁的举止让全家人都很难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncouth</td><td>/ʌnˈkuːθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无教养的</td><td>uncouth table manners<br>粗野的餐桌举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncouth remarks at dinner offended nearly everyone at the table.  他在晚餐上粗鲁的言辞几乎惹恼了同桌所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>usurp</b></td><td>/juːˈzɜːrp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>篡夺，篡位</td><td>usurp the throne<br>篡夺王位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general usurped power after the president fled.  总统逃走后，将军篡夺了权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrogate</td><td>/ˈærəˌɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>擅自占用，霸占</td><td>arrogate power to oneself<br>擅自把权力揽于己身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council arrogated to itself powers belonging to the mayor.  议会擅自把属于市长的权力揽为己有。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commandeer</td><td>/ˌkɑːmənˈdɪr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强行征用，强占</td><td>commandeer a vehicle<br>强行征用一辆车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soldiers commandeered every truck in the village that morning.  那天早晨士兵们征用了村里所有的卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convert</td><td>/kənˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>改变，转化</td><td>convert heat into energy<br>把热转化为能量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers converted the abandoned warehouse into a bright public library.  工程师们把废弃的仓库改造成了明亮的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expropriate</td><td>/eksˈproʊpriˌeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>征用，没收</td><td>expropriate private land<br>征收私人土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The state expropriated private land to build the new railway.  国家征收私人土地来修建新铁路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pirate</td><td>/ˈpaɪrət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>盗版，盗用</td><td>pirate a software program<br>盗版软件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company sued several websites for pirating its accounting software.  该公司起诉了几家盗版其会计软件的网站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preempt</td><td>/priˈempt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先占有</td><td>preempt the best seats<br>预先占住最好座位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early arrivals had preempted the best seats near the stage.  早到的人已经预先占住了靠舞台的最好座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 press</td><td>/ˈpres/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>按，压；催促</td><td>press the button<br>按下按钮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pressed the button twice before the lift moved.  他按了两次按钮电梯才动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seize</td><td>/ˈsiːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>领会，抓住（要点）</td><td>seize the main point<br>抓住要点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good students quickly seize the main point of a difficult lecture.  好学生能迅速抓住难懂讲座的要点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take over</td><td>/teɪk ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>接管，夺取控制</td><td>take over the company<br>接管公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A rival group took over the factory within a month.  一个竞争集团一个月内接管了这家工厂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abdicate</td><td>/ˈæbdɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>正式放弃（权力、责任）</td><td>abdicate the throne<br>退位，逊位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king chose to abdicate in favor of his younger brother.  国王选择退位让给他的弟弟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>univocal</b></del></td><td>/juːˈnɪvəkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义明确的</td><td>a univocal statement<br>含义明确的陈述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty needs univocal wording that no side can reinterpret later.  这份条约需要含义明确的措辞，任何一方都无法事后曲解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clear-cut</td><td>/ˌklɪrˈkʌt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，清晰的</td><td>a clear-cut decision<br>明确的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evidence gave us a clear-cut answer for the first time.  证据第一次给了我们明确的答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 definitive</td><td>/dɪˈfɪnɪtɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>确定的，不容置疑的；权威的</td><td>a definitive answer<br>确定无疑的答案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We still have no definitive answer from the committee.  我们仍未从委员会得到确定的答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unambiguous</td><td>/ˌʌnæmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，毫不含糊的</td><td>unambiguous written instructions<br>明确的书面指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract gives unambiguous rules about late payment.  合同就迟付款给出了明确规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unequivocal</td><td>/ˌʌnɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫不含糊的，明确的</td><td>an unequivocal denial<br>毫不含糊的否认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board issued an unequivocal statement supporting the director.  董事会发表了明确支持主任的声明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 explicit</td><td>/ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，明确的</td><td>explicit instructions<br>明确的指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave explicit instructions about how to store the samples.  她就样品的保存方式给出了明确指示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ambiguous</td><td>/æmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，含义不明的</td><td>an ambiguous reply<br>含糊其辞的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract's wording is ambiguous and invites future disputes.  合同措辞含义不明，容易引发日后争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indefinite</td><td>/ɪnˈdefənət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明确的，含糊的；无限期的</td><td>an indefinite answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They postponed the trip for an indefinite period without explaining why.  他们无限期推迟了旅行，也没解释原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inexplicit</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈsplɪsɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明确的，含糊的</td><td>inexplicit instructions<br>含糊的指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The inexplicit wording let both sides claim victory.  含糊的措辞让双方都宣称自己赢了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unspecific</td><td>/ˌʌnspəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不具体的，笼统的</td><td>unspecific complaints<br>笼统的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The warning was so unspecific that nobody knew which road to avoid.  警告太笼统，没人知道该避开哪条路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vague</td><td>/veɪɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达不清的</td><td>a vague answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vague answer told the reporters almost nothing about the plan.  他含糊的回答几乎没有向记者透露任何计划内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vaccinate</b></td><td>/ˈvæksɪneɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>预防接种疫苗</td><td>vaccinate against measles<br>接种麻疹疫苗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nurses vaccinated every child in the village against measles that week.  那一周护士为村里每个孩子接种了麻疹疫苗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immunize</td><td>/ˈɪmjuˌnaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使免疫，接种疫苗</td><td>immunize the children<br>为儿童接种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nurses immunized every child in the district by June.  护士在六月前为全区儿童完成了接种。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vacillate</b></td><td>/ˈvæsəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>犹豫不决；摇动，摇摆</td><td>vacillate between two options<br>在两个选择间犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parents who vacillate between approval and refusal confuse their children.  在同意与反对之间摇摆不定的父母会让孩子困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hesitate</td><td>/ˈhezəˌteɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>犹豫，迟疑</td><td>hesitate before answering<br>回答前犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not hesitate to call me if the plan changes.  如果计划有变，别犹豫，给我打电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dither</td><td>/ˈdɪðər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>紧张不安；犹豫不决</td><td>in a dither<br>慌乱不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was in a dither about which offer to accept.  他为该接受哪份工作邀约而慌乱不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waver</td><td>/ˈweɪvər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>动摇，犹豫不决</td><td>waver between two options<br>在两个选项间摇摆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wavered for weeks before accepting the job offer.  他犹豫了好几周才接受这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 teeter</td><td>/ˈtiːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，摇摇欲坠</td><td>teeter on the edge<br>在边缘摇摇欲坠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company teetered on the edge of bankruptcy for several months.  公司数月来一直在破产边缘摇摇欲坠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falter</td><td>/ˈfɔːltər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚；犹豫，踌躇</td><td>falter in his resolve<br>决心动摇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mr. Garrison never faltered in his resolve to finish the project.  加里森先生完成这个项目的决心从未动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oscillate</td><td>/ˈɑːsɪleɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摆，摆动；犹豫不定</td><td>oscillate between two views<br>在两种观点间摇摆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public opinion oscillated wildly during the long campaign.  漫长的竞选期间民意剧烈摇摆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluctuate</td><td>/ˈflʌktʃueɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>波动，起伏</td><td>fluctuate wildly<br>剧烈波动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Oil prices fluctuated wildly throughout the autumn months.  整个秋季油价剧烈波动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resolute</td><td>/ˈrezəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的</td><td>remain resolute<br>保持坚定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite heavy criticism, the mayor remained resolute about closing the old factory.  尽管备受批评，市长在关闭旧工厂一事上依然坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decide</td><td>/dɪˈsaɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>裁决，判定；决定</td><td>decide the case<br>对案件作出裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took the panel two days to decide who deserved the prize.  评审团花了两天才裁定谁该得奖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dive in</td><td>/ˈdaɪv ˌɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>果断投入，毫不迟疑地开始</td><td>dive in without hesitating<br>毫不犹豫地投入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rather than debate further, she dived in and started cooking.  她没再讨论，直接动手开始做饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plunge in</td><td>/ˈplʌndʒ ˌɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>一头扎进，果断行动</td><td>plunge in at once<br>立刻一头扎进去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He plunged in and repaired the wiring before anyone objected.  他一头扎进去，在有人反对前就修好了线路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 equipoise</td><td>/ˈekwəpɔɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平衡，均势</td><td>hold in equipoise<br>保持均势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two powers stayed in uneasy equipoise for a decade.  两大势力维持了十年不安的均势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vacuous</b></td><td>/ˈvækjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>茫然的，愚蠢的</td><td>a vacuous stare<br>茫然的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the difficult question with a vacuous stare and a shrug.  面对这个难题，他只是茫然地望着，耸了耸肩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 airhead</td><td>/ˈerˌhed/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋，没脑子的人</td><td>act like an airhead<br>表现得像个笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not treat her like an airhead; she reads constantly.  别把她当笨蛋，她一直在读书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brainless</td><td>/ˈbreɪnlɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，无脑的</td><td>a brainless mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Leaving the keys in the lock was simply brainless.  把钥匙留在锁上真是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dumb</td><td>/dʌm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的；不会说话的</td><td>a dumb question<br>愚蠢的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking that in public was a pretty dumb idea.  在公开场合问那个是个相当愚蠢的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fatuous</td><td>/ˈfætʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，昏庸的</td><td>a fatuous remark<br>愚蠢的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fatuous comment showed he had not read the report.  他愚蠢的评论说明他没读过那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foolish</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a foolish mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was foolish of him to ignore the warning signs.  他忽视警示标志实在愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inane</td><td>/ɪˈneɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>空洞的</td><td>inane chatter<br>空洞的闲聊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's dialogue was inane and endlessly repetitive.  这部电影的对白空洞且不断重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moronic</td><td>/məˈrɑnɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，白痴的</td><td>a moronic decision<br>愚蠢的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Driving without lights at night was simply moronic.  夜里不开灯行车实在愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obtuse</td><td>/əbˈtuːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚钝的</td><td>deliberately obtuse answers<br>故意装糊涂的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave deliberately obtuse answers to every question the inspector asked him.  他对检查员问的每个问题都故意给出装糊涂的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stupid</td><td>/ˈstuːpəd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，笨的</td><td>a stupid mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I felt stupid after mixing up their names again.  又把他们的名字弄混后我觉得自己很笨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 witless</td><td>/ˈwɪtləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，无头脑的</td><td>a witless remark<br>愚蠢的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witless prank got both boys suspended from school.  这个愚蠢的恶作剧让两个男孩都被停学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apt</td><td>/æpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，合适的；聪明的</td><td>an apt description<br>恰当的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is an apt description of how the meeting actually went.  那是对会议实际情况的一个恰当描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bright</td><td>/ˈbraɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快活的，开朗的；明亮的</td><td>a bright smile<br>开朗的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer with a bright and genuine smile.  她用开朗真诚的笑容迎接每位顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brilliant</td><td>/ˈbrɪljənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪颖的；辉煌的</td><td>a brilliant student<br>才华出众的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a brilliant mathematician long before she turned twenty.  她还没到二十岁就已是出色的数学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clever</td><td>/ˈklevər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，机敏的</td><td>a clever solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That was a clever way to avoid the extra cost.  那是避免额外成本的聪明办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intelligent</td><td>/ɪnˈtelədʒənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，聪明的</td><td>an intelligent decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the old plant was an intelligent decision.  卖掉旧厂是个明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 keen</td><td>/ˈkiːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏锐的，锐利的</td><td>a keen eye for detail<br>对细节的敏锐眼光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photographer has a keen eye for unusual light.  这位摄影师对特别的光线有敏锐的眼力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>vagary</b></del></td><td>/ˈveɪɡəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不可预测的思想或行为</td><td>the vagaries of fashion<br>时尚的无常变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small farmers still suffer from the vagaries of weather and market prices.  小农户仍受天气与市场价格无常变化之苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 caprice</td><td>/kəˈpriːs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>反复无常，一时兴起</td><td>at the caprice of<br>任凭…一时兴起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The schedule changed daily at the caprice of the new director.  日程随新主管的一时兴起天天变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fancy</td><td>/ˈfænsi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>喜爱，想要</td><td>fancy a cup of tea<br>想喝杯茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do you fancy a walk along the river this evening?  今晚想不想去河边散散步？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 freak</td><td>/ˈfriːk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热爱好者；怪人</td><td>a fitness freak<br>健身狂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My younger brother is a complete film freak.  我弟弟是个十足的电影狂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humor</td><td>/ˈhjuːmər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>迁就；顺应；纵容</td><td>humor a small child<br>顺着小孩的心意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She humored her grandfather by listening to the same story again.  她顺着祖父的心意，又听了一遍同样的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vagrancy</td><td>/ˈveɪɡrənsi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（思绪）游移不定；流浪</td><td>the vagrancy of his thoughts<br>他思绪的游移不定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vagrancy of his thoughts made the long lecture hard to follow.  他思绪的游移不定让这场长讲座难以跟上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whimsy</td><td>/ˈwɪmsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>奇思怪想，异想天开</td><td>a touch of whimsy<br>一点奇思妙想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden's stone frogs add a note of whimsy.  花园里的石蛙添了一点奇趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vague</b></td><td>/veɪɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达不清的</td><td>a vague answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vague answer told the reporters almost nothing about the plan.  他含糊的回答几乎没有向记者透露任何计划内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambiguous</td><td>/æmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，含义不明的</td><td>an ambiguous reply<br>含糊其辞的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract's wording is ambiguous and invites future disputes.  合同措辞含义不明，容易引发日后争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indefinite</td><td>/ɪnˈdefənət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明确的，含糊的；无限期的</td><td>an indefinite answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They postponed the trip for an indefinite period without explaining why.  他们无限期推迟了旅行，也没解释原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inexplicit</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈsplɪsɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明确的，含糊的</td><td>inexplicit instructions<br>含糊的指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The inexplicit wording let both sides claim victory.  含糊的措辞让双方都宣称自己赢了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unclear</td><td>/ʌnˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不清楚的，不明确的</td><td>remain unclear<br>仍不明确</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is still unclear who made the final decision.  谁做的最终决定仍不清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dim</td><td>/ˈdɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>暗淡的，模糊的</td><td>a dim light<br>昏暗的灯光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was too dim for reading anything but large print.  房间太暗，只能看大字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faint</td><td>/ˈfeɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；微弱的</td><td>a faint voice<br>微弱的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt faint after standing in the sun too long.  在太阳下站太久后她感到虚弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foggy</td><td>/ˈfɑːɡi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的；有雾的</td><td>a foggy memory<br>模糊的记忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My memory of that evening is still rather foggy.  我对那晚的记忆仍相当模糊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hazy</td><td>/ˈheɪzi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，朦胧的</td><td>a hazy recollection<br>模糊的记忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I have only a hazy recollection of that summer.  我对那个夏天只有模糊的记忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indistinct</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊不清的，含糊的</td><td>an indistinct outline<br>模糊的轮廓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph shows an indistinct figure by the door.  照片上门边有个模糊的人影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indistinguishable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法区分的，难以辨别的</td><td>indistinguishable from the original<br>与原件无法区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The copy was almost indistinguishable from the original painting.  这份复制品与原画几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misty</td><td>/ˈmɪsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朦胧的，模糊不清的</td><td>misty outlines<br>朦胧的轮廓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The misty hills beyond the lake looked almost unreal.  湖那边朦胧的山丘看着近乎不真实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 murky</td><td>/ˈmɜːrki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏暗的，阴暗的；晦涩不清的</td><td>a murky, windowless basement<br>昏暗无窗的地下室</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The murky basement was lit by one failing bulb.  昏暗的地下室里只有一只快坏的灯泡照明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nebulous</td><td>/ˈnebjələs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的</td><td>a nebulous plan<br>模糊不清的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager presented a nebulous plan with no dates, budgets, or clear goals.  经理提出了一个没有日期、预算或明确目标的模糊计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opaque</td><td>/oʊˈpeɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不透明的；晦涩难懂的</td><td>opaque legal language<br>晦涩的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regulations remain opaque even to experienced tax lawyers.  这些法规连资深税务律师都觉得晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 definite</td><td>/ˈdefənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确定的</td><td>a definite answer<br>明确的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need a definite answer before we can book the venue.  我们需要明确答复才能订场地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 express</td><td>/ɪkˈspres/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>表达，表现；快速的</td><td>express deep gratitude<br>表达深切感谢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She struggled to express the gratitude she felt toward her mentor.  她难以表达对导师的感激之情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 explicit</td><td>/ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，明确的</td><td>explicit instructions<br>明确的指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave explicit instructions about how to store the samples.  她就样品的保存方式给出了明确指示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 specific</td><td>/spəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，特定的；专门的</td><td>a specific example<br>一个具体的例子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Can you give me a specific example of the problem?  你能给我一个这个问题的具体例子吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>valediction</b></td><td>/ˌvælɪˈdɪkʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>告别词</td><td>a brief valediction<br>简短的告别词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old teacher offered a brief valediction and left the crowded hall.  老教师说了简短的告别词，便离开了拥挤的礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>valiant</b></td><td>/ˈvæliənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的，英勇的</td><td>a valiant effort<br>英勇的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A valiant firefighter carried two children out of the burning building.  一位英勇的消防员把两个孩子抱出了燃烧的大楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brave</td><td>/ˈbreɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的</td><td>a brave decision<br>勇敢的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was brave of her to speak against the new policy.  她敢于反对新政策，很勇敢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 courageous</td><td>/kəˈreɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的，有胆量的</td><td>a courageous decision<br>勇敢的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was courageous of him to speak out against the policy.  他公开反对这项政策，很有勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dauntless</td><td>/ˈdɔntləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，勇敢的</td><td>dauntless courage<br>无畏的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dauntless nurse worked through shelling to reach the wounded.  这位无畏的护士在炮击中赶去救伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fearless</td><td>/ˈfɪrləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，大胆的</td><td>a fearless reporter<br>无畏的记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was fearless in questioning the most powerful men present.  她敢于质问在场最有权势的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gallant</td><td>/ˈɡælənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英勇的</td><td>a gallant defense<br>英勇的防守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallant paratroopers jumped from the plane without a moment's hesitation.  英勇的伞兵们毫不犹豫地跳出了飞机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrepid</td><td>/ɪnˈtrepɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的</td><td>an intrepid explorer<br>无畏的探险家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The intrepid explorer crossed the frozen strait alone in the middle of winter.  这位大胆的探险家在隆冬时节独自穿越了结冰的海峡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stalwart</td><td>/ˈstɔːlwərt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定忠诚的；强壮的</td><td>a stalwart supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained a stalwart supporter of the project through every setback.  历经挫折，她始终是这个项目的坚定支持者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stout</td><td>/ˈstaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，勇敢的；粗壮的</td><td>stout resistance<br>顽强的抵抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The townspeople put up a stout defense against the raiders.  镇民对袭击者进行了顽强的抵抗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 valorous</td><td>/ˈvælərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇猛的，英勇的</td><td>a valorous knight<br>英勇的骑士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Songs still honor the valorous soldiers who held the bridge.  歌曲仍在颂扬守住那座桥的英勇士兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cowardly</td><td>/ˈkaʊərdli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>怯懦的，胆小的</td><td>a cowardly attack<br>卑怯的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cowardly to blame the mistake on his assistant.  把错误推给助手是怯懦的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 craven</td><td>/ˈkreɪvn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常懦弱的，因胆小而遭人鄙视的</td><td>a craven retreat<br>怯懦的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His craven silence during the trial disappointed everyone who had trusted him.  他在庭审中懦弱的沉默让所有信任过他的人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gutless</td><td>/ˈɡʌtləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没胆量的，懦弱的</td><td>a gutless decision<br>懦弱的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team looked gutless in the second half of the match.  球队下半场表现得毫无斗志。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nerveless</td><td>/ˈnɜːrvləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>软弱无力的；镇定的</td><td>nerveless fingers<br>软弱无力的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cup slipped from his nerveless fingers and broke.  杯子从他无力的手指间滑落打碎了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pusillanimous</td><td>/ˌpjuːsɪˈlænɪməs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的，懦弱的</td><td>a pusillanimous response<br>软弱的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the government's pusillanimous silence a betrayal of victims.  批评者称政府懦弱的沉默是对受害者的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spineless</td><td>/ˈspaɪnləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没骨气的，软弱的</td><td>a spineless coward<br>没骨气的胆小鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too spineless to stand up to his own manager.  他太没骨气，不敢反抗自己的经理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timorous</td><td>/ˈtɪmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的</td><td>a timorous little voice<br>胆怯的小声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The timorous student finally raised her hand to answer the question.  那个胆怯的学生终于举手回答问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>versed</b></td><td>/vɜːrst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟知的；精通的</td><td>well versed in history<br>精通历史</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is well versed in both classical and modern poetry.  他对古典诗歌和现代诗歌都很精通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abreast</td><td>/əˈbrest/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>副词、形容词</td><td>熟知的</td><td>keep abreast of the news<br>及时了解新闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reads three newspapers daily to keep abreast of political developments.  她每天读三份报纸，以便及时了解政治动态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acquainted</td><td>/əˈkweɪntɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，认识的</td><td>be acquainted with<br>熟悉；与……相识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I am not well acquainted with the rules of this game.  我对这个游戏的规则不太熟悉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conversant</td><td>/kənˈvɜːrsnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，通晓的</td><td>conversant with the law<br>通晓法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is fully conversant with the new safety regulations.  她非常熟悉新的安全条例。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 informed</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrmd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>见识广的，掌握信息的</td><td>make an informed decision<br>做出明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters need accurate data to make an informed choice.  选民需要准确的数据才能做出明智的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knowledgeable</td><td>/ˈnɑːlədʒəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>知识丰富的，了解很多的</td><td>knowledgeable about wine<br>懂葡萄酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our guide was remarkably knowledgeable about local birds.  我们的导游对当地鸟类了解得非常多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-informed</td><td>/ˌwel ɪnˈfɔrmd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>见闻广博的，消息灵通的</td><td>a well-informed source<br>消息可靠的来源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A well-informed reader spotted the error in the report.  一位见闻广博的读者发现了报告中的错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accomplished</td><td>/əˈkɑːmplɪʃt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺精湛的，有造诣的</td><td>an accomplished pianist<br>技艺精湛的钢琴家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is an accomplished pianist who has toured three continents.  她是位技艺精湛的钢琴家，已在三大洲巡演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adept</td><td>/əˈdept/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>内行的，熟练的；行家</td><td>adept at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is remarkably adept at calming angry customers.  她极擅长安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complete</td><td>/kəmˈpliːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>完全的，彻底的；完成</td><td>a complete failure<br>彻底的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The first attempt was a complete failure.  第一次尝试彻底失败了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consummate</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsəmət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专业的，有造诣的；无纰漏的，完美的</td><td>a consummate professional<br>极为专业的行家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the hostile interview with the ease of a consummate diplomat.  她像老练的外交家一样从容应对了这场充满敌意的采访。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 experienced</td><td>/ɪkˈspɪriənst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有经验的，老练的</td><td>an experienced guide<br>经验丰富的导游</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We hired an experienced nurse to care for my grandmother.  我们请了一位有经验的护士照顾我祖母。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expert</td><td>/ˈekspərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>专家；熟练的，专业的</td><td>a leading expert<br>顶尖专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called in an expert to examine the cracked foundation.  他们请来专家检查开裂的地基。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 master</td><td>/ˈmæstər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>大师，名家；主人</td><td>a master of disguise<br>伪装大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a master of the craft could carve such fine details.  只有行家高手才能雕出这般精细的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 practiced</td><td>/ˈpræktəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，老练的</td><td>a practiced hand<br>熟练的能手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With practiced ease she filleted the fish in seconds.  她熟练自如，几秒钟就把鱼剔了骨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 professed</td><td>/prəˈfest/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自称的，公开声称的</td><td>a professed expert<br>自称的专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professed authority could not answer a basic question.  这位自称的权威连基本问题都答不上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skilled</td><td>/ˈskɪld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，有技能的</td><td>skilled labor<br>熟练劳动力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory struggles to find skilled workers for its new assembly line.  工厂难以为新装配线找到熟练工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veteran</td><td>/ˈvetərən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老兵；有丰富经验的人</td><td>a veteran journalist<br>资深记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A veteran journalist, she has covered four wars and six elections.  作为资深记者，她报道过四场战争和六次选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virtuoso</td><td>/ˌvɜːrtʃuˈoʊsoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>技艺大师，演奏名家；技艺精湛的</td><td>a violin virtuoso<br>小提琴演奏大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The violin virtuoso played the difficult passage without a single mistake.  那位小提琴大师毫无差错地演奏了这段难曲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignorant</td><td>/ˈɪɡnərənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知的，不知道的</td><td>ignorant of the facts<br>不了解事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They were completely ignorant of the danger ahead.  他们对前方的危险毫不知情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unacquainted</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈkweɪntɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟悉的，不了解的</td><td>unacquainted with the area<br>不熟悉这个地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Being unacquainted with the city, he got lost very quickly.  由于不熟悉这座城市，他很快就迷路了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfamiliar</td><td>/ˌʌnfəˈmɪljər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟悉的，陌生的</td><td>an unfamiliar face<br>一张陌生的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The street names here were all unfamiliar to me.  这里的街名对我来说都很陌生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uninformed</td><td>/ˌʌnɪnˈfɔːrmd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不了解情况的，无知的</td><td>an uninformed opinion<br>不明情况的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Uninformed criticism rarely helps anyone improve their work.  不了解情况的批评很少能帮助别人改进工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unknowledgeable</del></td><td>/ˌʌnˈnɑlɪdʒəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏知识的，不懂行的</td><td>unknowledgeable about finance<br>不懂金融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt unknowledgeable about finance and hired an adviser.  他觉得自己不懂金融，于是聘请了一位顾问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amateur</td><td>/ˈæməˌtɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>业余爱好者；外行的</td><td>a talented amateur<br>有天赋的业余者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comet was first spotted by an amateur astronomer.  这颗彗星最先由一位业余天文学家发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inexperienced</td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈspɪriənst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏经验的，不熟练的</td><td>an inexperienced driver<br>缺乏经验的司机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team was young and inexperienced but full of energy.  这支队伍年轻缺乏经验，却充满活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inexpert</td><td>/ˌɪnˈekspɜːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，外行的</td><td>inexpert hands<br>不熟练的手法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 To inexpert eyes the two paintings looked exactly the same.  在外行眼里，这两幅画看起来完全一样。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 jackleg</del></td><td>/ˈdʒækˌlɛɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>外行的；蹩脚的（人）</td><td>a jackleg carpenter<br>蹩脚的木匠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some jackleg plumber installed all these pipes backwards years ago.  多年前某个蹩脚水管工把这些管子全装反了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unprofessional</td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈfeʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不专业的，外行的</td><td>unprofessional conduct<br>不专业的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arriving late to every meeting looks deeply unprofessional.  每次会议都迟到显得极不专业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unseasoned</td><td>/ʌnˈsizənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏经验的；未调味的</td><td>unseasoned recruits<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team fielded unseasoned players against the reigning champions.  球队派出毫无经验的球员迎战卫冕冠军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unskilled</td><td>/ənˈskɪld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，无技术的</td><td>unskilled labor<br>非技术劳工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of these jobs are unskilled and pay barely above the minimum.  这些工作多为非技术岗，薪水刚过最低标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vanquish</b></td><td>/ˈvæŋkwɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打败，征服</td><td>vanquish the invading army<br>击败入侵的军队</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A smaller force managed to vanquish the invading army in one day.  一支人数较少的部队一天之内就击败了入侵的军队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conquer</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>征服，攻克</td><td>conquer a vast territory<br>征服广阔领土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rome conquered most of the Mediterranean world within two centuries.  罗马在两个世纪内征服了地中海世界的大部分地区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dominate</td><td>/ˈdɑːməˌneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>支配，统治；俯视</td><td>dominate the market<br>支配市场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two firms dominate the industry and set nearly every price.  两家公司支配着这个行业，几乎决定所有价格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overpower</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈpaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>制服，压倒</td><td>overpower the guards<br>制服守卫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three officers finally overpowered the struggling suspect in the hallway.  三名警官最终在走廊里制服了挣扎的嫌疑人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subdue</td><td>/səbˈduː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使顺从；征服</td><td>subdue the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Government forces moved south to subdue the rebellion within a week.  政府军南下，一周内镇压了叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subject</td><td>/ˈsʌbdʒekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>取决于（其他因素）的，受制于</td><td>subject to approval<br>须经批准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The entire plan is subject to approval by the city council.  整个方案须经市议会批准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subjugate</td><td>/ˈsʌbdʒuɡeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>征服，镇压</td><td>subjugate a rebellious province<br>征服叛乱的行省</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire sent legions to subjugate a rebellious northern province.  帝国派出军团征服叛乱的北方行省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subordinate</td><td>/səˈbɔːrdənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>次要的，下级的；使服从</td><td>a subordinate role<br>次要的角色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to accept a subordinate role in the research project.  她不愿在研究项目中接受次要的角色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 capitulate</td><td>/kəˈpɪtʃuleɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>投降，默许</td><td>capitulate to their demands<br>屈从他们的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After weeks of protest the company capitulated to the workers' demands.  抗议数周后，公司向工人的要求屈服了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 surrender</td><td>/səˈrendər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>交出，放弃，投降</td><td>surrender to the police<br>向警方投降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief finally surrendered to the police after a long chase.  经过长时间追捕，小偷终于向警方投降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yield</td><td>/ˈjiːld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>产生，出产；屈服，让出</td><td>yield results<br>产生结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months of patient testing finally yielded a usable vaccine.  数月耐心的试验终于产出可用的疫苗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vapid</b></td><td>/ˈvæpɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的，乏味的</td><td>a vapid conversation<br>乏味的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party ended in vapid conversation about traffic and the weather.  聚会最后只剩下关于交通和天气的乏味闲谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 driveling</del></td><td>/ˈdrɪvəlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>说傻话的，胡扯的</td><td>driveling nonsense<br>愚蠢的胡话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the report as driveling nonsense from amateurs.  他把这份报告斥为业余者的愚蠢胡话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flat</td><td>/ˈflæt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调无光的，乏味的</td><td>a flat performance<br>平淡无味的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paint dried to a flat, lifeless gray.  油漆干成了一种无光而死板的灰色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inane</td><td>/ɪˈneɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>空洞的</td><td>inane chatter<br>空洞的闲聊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's dialogue was inane and endlessly repetitive.  这部电影的对白空洞且不断重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insipid</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪpɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡的，无聊的</td><td>an insipid conversation<br>无聊的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film was so insipid that half the audience fell asleep.  这部电影平淡无聊，半数观众都睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jejune</td><td>/dʒɪˈdʒuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣乏味的</td><td>a jejune argument<br>乏味浅薄的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the sequel as jejune and repetitive.  评论家认为这部续集乏味且重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sapless</del></td><td>/ˈsæpləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无活力的，枯干的</td><td>a sapless performance<br>毫无生气的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old comedian gave a sapless performance that bored everyone.  这位老喜剧演员的表演毫无生气，让所有人都很无聊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tasteless</td><td>/ˈteɪstləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无味的；乏味的</td><td>tasteless soup<br>淡而无味的汤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hospital food was warm but completely tasteless.  医院的饭是热的，却全然无味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorbing</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，吸引人的</td><td>an absorbing novel<br>引人入胜的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found her account of the voyage utterly absorbing.  我觉得她对航行的叙述极其引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arresting</td><td>/əˈrestɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an arresting image<br>引人注目的画面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poster used an arresting image of a burning city.  海报用了一幅燃烧城市的醒目画面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engrossing</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，令人专注的</td><td>an engrossing story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary was so engrossing that nobody left early.  这部纪录片如此引人入胜，没人提早离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enthralling</td><td>/ɪnˈθrɔlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人入迷的，极吸引人的</td><td>an enthralling performance<br>令人入迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enthralling storytelling kept the children silent for an hour.  她迷人的讲述让孩子们安静了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fascinating</td><td>/ˈfæsəˌneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，极有趣的</td><td>a fascinating discovery<br>引人入胜的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Insect behavior is a fascinating subject for young scientists.  昆虫行为对年轻科学家来说是极有趣的课题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immersing</td><td>/ɪˈmɜrsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人沉浸的，投入的</td><td>an immersing experience<br>令人沉浸的体验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The game offers an immersing world full of hidden details.  这款游戏提供了充满隐藏细节的沉浸世界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intriguing</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtriːɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人好奇的，有趣的</td><td>an intriguing question<br>引人好奇的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an intriguing point that nobody had considered.  她提出了一个无人考虑过的有趣观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vaporize</b></td><td>/ˈveɪpəraɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底消灭</td><td>vaporize the target<br>把目标彻底毁灭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single blast vaporized the abandoned tower on the distant hill.  一次爆炸就把远处山上那座废弃塔楼彻底摧毁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annihilate</td><td>/əˈnaɪəˌleɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底摧毁，歼灭</td><td>annihilate the enemy<br>歼灭敌人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single flood could annihilate the entire harvest of the valley.  一场洪水就能彻底毁掉整个山谷的收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decimate</td><td>/ˈdesəˌmeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>大量毁灭，大批杀死</td><td>decimate the population<br>使人口大量减少</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The epidemic decimated herds of cattle across three provinces.  这场疫病使三个省的牛群大量死亡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demolish</td><td>/dɪˈmɑːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拆除，摧毁；驳倒</td><td>demolish an old building<br>拆除旧楼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city decided to demolish the unsafe old building last spring.  去年春天市政府决定拆除这栋危旧建筑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devastate</td><td>/ˈdevəˌsteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>毁灭，破坏；使震惊</td><td>devastate the coastal town<br>摧毁那座海滨小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The earthquake devastated neighborhoods that had stood for a century.  地震摧毁了矗立了一个世纪的街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extinguish</td><td>/ɪkˈstɪŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>熄灭</td><td>extinguish the fire<br>扑灭大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to extinguish the burning warehouse.  消防员整夜工作扑灭燃烧的仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nuke</td><td>/ˈnuːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>用核武器攻击；彻底摧毁</td><td>nuke a city<br>对一座城市发动核打击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Generals debated whether to nuke the island rather than invade it.  将军们争论是否用核武器打击该岛而非登陆进攻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pulverize</td><td>/ˈpʌlvəraɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>将…研磨成粉；将…彻底摧毁</td><td>pulverize the rock<br>把岩石磨成粉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers used heavy hammers to pulverize the rock into fine powder.  工人用重锤把岩石研磨成细粉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raze</td><td>/reɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底摧毁，拆为平地</td><td>raze a village<br>将村庄彻底摧毁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invading army razed the village and burned every field around it.  入侵军队将村庄彻底摧毁，并烧掉了周围所有田地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruin</td><td>/ˈruːɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>毁坏，毁灭；废墟</td><td>ruin the harvest<br>毁掉收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of drought ruined the harvest and emptied the village granary.  数周干旱毁掉收成，村里粮仓也空了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smash</td><td>/ˈsmæʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>轰动的成功；打碎</td><td>a box-office smash<br>票房大热之作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The low-budget comedy became a box-office smash overnight.  这部低成本喜剧一夜之间成为票房大热。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wreck</td><td>/ˈrek/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>残骸；失事；破坏</td><td>the wreck of a ship<br>一艘船的残骸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Divers photographed the wreck lying deep on the sandy bottom.  潜水员拍摄了深卧沙底的残骸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 build</td><td>/ˈbɪld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>建造，建立；体格</td><td>build a bridge<br>建造一座桥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They plan to build a new library on the empty lot downtown.  他们计划在市中心的空地上建一座新图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 construct</td><td>/kənˈstrʌkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>建造，建立；创造，想出</td><td>construct a theory<br>构建一套理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer constructed a careful argument from a handful of ordinary documents.  律师用少量普通文件构建出一套严密的论证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 erect</td><td>/ɪˈrekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>直立的，垂直的</td><td>stand erect<br>笔直地站着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old soldier still stood perfectly erect.  这位老兵仍站得笔直。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 establish</td><td>/ɪˈstæblɪʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>确证，证实；建立</td><td>establish the facts beyond doubt<br>确证事实无疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The investigation established the facts beyond any reasonable doubt.  调查毫无疑问地确证了事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 raise</td><td>/ˈreɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；举起，提高</td><td>raise an objection<br>提出反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised an objection just before the vote was taken.  就在投票前他提出了反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rear</td><td>/ˈrɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>养育，培养；竖立，后部</td><td>rear a large family<br>养育一大家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had to rear four children alone after her husband died.  丈夫去世后她只能独自养育四个孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>variance</b></td><td>/ˈveriəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不一致，不和谐</td><td>at variance with<br>与…不一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account was clearly at variance with the official police report.  他的说法明显与警方的官方报告不一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conflict</td><td>/ˈkɑːnflɪkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冲突，矛盾；抵触</td><td>a conflict of interest<br>利益冲突</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge withdrew because of a possible conflict of interest.  法官因可能存在利益冲突而回避。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disaccord</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈkɔrd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>不一致，不和</td><td>in disaccord with the plan<br>与计划不一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His testimony was in clear disaccord with the written record.  他的证词与书面记录明显不一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discordance</td><td>/dɪsˈkɔːrdəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不一致，不和</td><td>discordance between reports<br>各报告之间的矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a clear discordance between the two witnesses' accounts.  两位证人的叙述之间存在明显不一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disharmony</td><td>/dɪsˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不和谐，不一致</td><td>racial disharmony<br>种族间的不和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of disharmony finally split the once famous band.  多年的不和最终使这支曾经著名的乐队解散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissent</td><td>/dɪˈsent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>持异议，不同意；反对正统</td><td>voice one's dissent<br>表达异议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two judges dissented from the majority opinion of the court.  两位法官对法院的多数意见持异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 division</td><td>/dɪˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不和；分开，部门</td><td>deep division over policy<br>政策上的深刻分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is deep division within the committee about the budget.  委员会内部在预算上存在深刻分歧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 friction</td><td>/ˈfrɪkʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>摩擦；冲突，不和</td><td>friction between them<br>他们之间的摩擦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant friction between the two managers slowed the whole project.  两位经理之间不断的摩擦拖慢了整个项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 schism</td><td>/ˈskɪzəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，派系对立</td><td>a bitter schism<br>严重的分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bitter schism split the party into two hostile factions.  一场激烈的分裂把该党撕成两个敌对派系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 variation</td><td>/ˌveriˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>变化，变体</td><td>slight variation in color<br>颜色的细微变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is wide variation in rainfall across the region.  该地区降雨量差异很大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 war</td><td>/ˈwɔːr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>冲突，斗争；战争</td><td>a price war<br>价格战</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A price war between the two airlines cut fares sharply.  两家航空公司的价格战大幅压低了票价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accord</td><td>/əˈkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一致，符合；授予</td><td>in accord with<br>与…一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His actions were in accord with the policy he announced.  他的行动与他宣布的政策一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agreement</td><td>/əˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，同意；协议</td><td>come to an agreement<br>达成协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two firms signed an agreement to share research costs.  两家公司签署协议分担研究费用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concord</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，和睦</td><td>live in concord<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty brought decades of concord between the two states.  该条约给两国带来数十年的和睦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concordance</td><td>/kənˈkɔrdəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，和谐；索引</td><td>in concordance with the data<br>与数据一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The results show close concordance with earlier field studies.  结果显示与早期实地研究高度一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmony</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和谐，融洽</td><td>live in harmony with<br>与…和谐共处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peace</td><td>/ˈpiːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和平，和睦；平静</td><td>live in peace<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
