<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two villages have lived in peace for over a century.  这两个村庄和睦相处已逾一个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>variegated</b></td><td>/ˈverɪɡeɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杂色的，斑驳的</td><td>variegated leaves<br>斑驳的叶子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She filled the garden with variegated leaves of green and cream.  她在花园里种满绿白斑驳的叶子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chromatic</td><td>/krəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>彩色的</td><td>brilliant chromatic patterns<br>绚丽的彩色图案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quilt showed brilliant chromatic patterns across every square.  这条被子每一格都是绚丽的彩色图案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kaleidoscopic</td><td>/kəˌlaɪdəˈskɑpɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>千变万化的；万花筒般的</td><td>a kaleidoscopic display<br>千变万化的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The festival offered a kaleidoscopic mix of music and costume.  节庆呈现出千变万化的音乐与服饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 iridescent</td><td>/ˌɪrɪˈdesnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>五颜六色的</td><td>iridescent butterfly wings<br>虹彩般的蝶翅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sunlight made the butterfly's iridescent wings flash green, blue, and gold.  阳光让蝴蝶五颜六色的翅膀闪出绿色、蓝色和金色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 motley</td><td>/ˈmɑːtli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混杂的，富于变化的</td><td>a motley crowd<br>混杂的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A motley crowd of farmers, students, and shopkeepers filled the town square.  农民、学生和店主组成的混杂人群挤满了市镇广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rainbow</td><td>/ˈreɪnˌboʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>五彩缤纷的；彩虹</td><td>rainbow colors<br>缤纷的色彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A rainbow of umbrellas filled the square during the rain.  雨中广场上满是五彩缤纷的雨伞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 monochromatic</td><td>/ˌmɑːnəkroʊˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单色的</td><td>a monochromatic palette<br>单色的配色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The designer chose a monochromatic scheme of soft grays.  设计师选了柔灰的单色方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>varnish</b></td><td>/ˈvɑːrnɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>粉饰（令人不悦的东西）</td><td>varnish the truth<br>粉饰真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The official tried to varnish the truth about the failed project.  那位官员试图粉饰这个失败项目的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blanch</td><td>/ˈblæntʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使变白，漂白；发白</td><td>blanch the almonds<br>把杏仁焯白</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blanched visibly when the bill was handed over.  账单递来时他明显脸色发白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 extenuate</del></td><td>/ɪkˈstenjueɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻罪过</td><td>extenuate the offense<br>减轻罪责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His youth did not extenuate so serious an offense.  他年纪轻并不能减轻如此严重的罪责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloss</td><td>/ɡlɑːs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>简短解释；敷衍潦草做事（甚至忽略）</td><td>gloss over the problem<br>敷衍带过问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor added a short gloss explaining the unfamiliar legal term.  编者加了一条简短的解释，说明这个陌生的法律术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 palliate</del></td><td>/ˈpælieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，缓和；掩饰</td><td>palliate the symptoms<br>缓解症状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new drug can only palliate the pain, not cure the disease.  这种新药只能减轻疼痛，无法治愈疾病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sugarcoat</td><td>/ˈʃʊɡərkoʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>粉饰，美化</td><td>sugarcoat bad news<br>粉饰坏消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor refused to sugarcoat the test results.  医生不愿粉饰检查结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veneer</td><td>/vəˈnɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>虚饰，假象；薄薄的表层</td><td>a veneer of politeness<br>一层礼貌的虚饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His charm was only a veneer over real cruelty.  他的魅力只是掩盖真正残忍的一层虚饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bare</td><td>/ˈber/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>揭露，袒露；赤裸的</td><td>bare the truth<br>揭露真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the interview he bared his family's darkest secrets.  在采访中他揭露了家族最黑暗的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disclose</td><td>/dɪsˈkloʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，公开，透露</td><td>disclose the details<br>公开细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to disclose the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝公开和解协议的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discover</td><td>/dɪˈskʌvər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>发现；揭露</td><td>discover a hidden flaw<br>发现隐藏缺陷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Auditors discovered that the accounts had been altered.  审计员发现账目被改动过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 divulge</td><td>/daɪˈvʌldʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>泄漏（秘密）</td><td>divulge a secret<br>泄露秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No employee may divulge a secret learned during the merger negotiations.  任何员工都不得泄露在并购谈判中得知的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expose</td><td>/ɪkˈspoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露；使暴露</td><td>expose the fraud<br>揭露欺诈行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two journalists exposed the fraud after months of quiet investigation.  两名记者经过数月暗中调查揭露了这起欺诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reveal</td><td>/rɪˈviːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭示，透露</td><td>reveal the details<br>透露细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to reveal the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝透露和解的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncloak</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkloʊk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，揭开真相</td><td>uncloak a conspiracy<br>揭露阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist worked for months to uncloak the corruption scheme.  这位记者花了数月揭露这个腐败阴谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncover</td><td>/ənˈkʌvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭开，发现，揭露</td><td>uncover the evidence<br>发现证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors uncovered evidence of fraud in the accounting records.  检查人员在账目中发现了欺诈的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unmask</td><td>/ʌnˈmæsk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，揭穿真面目</td><td>unmask the impostor<br>揭穿那个冒充者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist unmasked the company's secret polluting practices.  记者揭露了这家公司秘密排污的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unveil</td><td>/ənˈveɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭幕，公布，揭示</td><td>unveil a new model<br>发布新款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company will unveil a new model at the spring show.  公司将在春季展会上发布新款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>valorize</b></del></td><td>/ˈvæləraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>提升，赞美</td><td>valorize local traditions<br>推崇本地传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum's new exhibit valorizes the crafts of ordinary village women.  博物馆的新展览推崇普通乡村妇女的手工技艺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exalt</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬；提升（地位）</td><td>exalt to the skies<br>推崇到极点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics exalt her early novels as masterpieces of the era.  评论家将她早期的小说赞为那个时代的杰作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>vaunt</b></del></td><td>/vɔːnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>吹嘘</td><td>vaunt one's achievements<br>夸耀自己的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He vaunted his prizes until even his friends stopped listening.  他不停吹嘘自己的奖项，连朋友都不再听了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boast</td><td>/ˈboʊst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>自夸，吹嘘</td><td>boast about his wealth<br>吹嘘自己的财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He boasted about the deal long before it was signed.  合同还没签他就开始吹嘘这笔交易。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brag</td><td>/ˈbræɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>自夸，吹牛</td><td>brag about one's wealth<br>炫耀自己的财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bragged about his new car until everyone stopped listening.  他一直吹嘘自己的新车，直到大家都不再听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brandish</td><td>/ˈbrændɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（带有威胁性地）挥舞</td><td>brandish a sword<br>挥舞长剑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The angry pirate began to brandish a sword above his head.  愤怒的海盗开始在头顶挥舞长剑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swagger</td><td>/ˈswæɡər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>趾高气扬地走；自夸</td><td>swagger about one's success<br>炫耀自己的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He swaggered into the room after winning the bowling championship.  赢得保龄球锦标赛后，他大摇大摆地走进房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 belittle</td><td>/bɪˈlɪtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低，轻视</td><td>belittle her achievements<br>贬低她的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't belittle her achievements just because she works quietly.  别因为她默默工作就贬低她的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deprecate</td><td>/ˈdeprəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不赞成，反对；贬低</td><td>deprecate his efforts<br>贬低他的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tends to deprecate his own achievements whenever anyone praises them.  每当有人称赞，他总倾向于贬低自己的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diminish</td><td>/dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）减少；轻视，贬低</td><td>diminish in importance<br>重要性下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His influence diminished steadily after he left the board.  他离开董事会后影响力持续减弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 minimize</td><td>/ˈmɪnɪmaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意低估</td><td>minimize the risks<br>刻意淡化风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company tried to minimize the risks in its statement to worried investors.  公司在给焦虑投资者的声明中试图刻意低估风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 underrate</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>低估，看轻</td><td>underrate an opponent<br>轻视对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics badly underrated her first novel at the time.  评论界当时严重低估了她的第一部小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undervalue</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈvæljuː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>低估价值，看轻</td><td>undervalue her contribution<br>低估她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company undervalued its most experienced engineers for years.  公司多年来低估了最有经验的工程师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>veer</b></td><td>/vɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）转向，（使）改变航线</td><td>veer off course<br>偏离航线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old boat began to veer sharply off course in the wind.  那艘旧船在风中开始急剧偏离航线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avert</td><td>/əˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，防止；转移（目光）</td><td>avert a disaster<br>避免灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Quick action by the crew averted a serious accident.  船员的迅速行动避免了一场严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deviate</td><td>/ˈdiːviˌeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>偏离，背离</td><td>deviate from the plan<br>偏离计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot had to deviate from the planned route because of storms.  因风暴，飞行员不得不偏离既定航线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deflect</td><td>/dɪˈflekt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使偏斜，使转向；挡开，避开</td><td>deflect the criticism<br>挡开批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The politician deflected every awkward question with a well practiced joke.  那位政客用一个练熟的玩笑挡开每个尴尬的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 divert</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使转向；使消遣</td><td>divert attention from<br>转移对…的注意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise diverted my attention from the lecture.  噪音把我的注意力从讲座上引开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 redirect</td><td>/ˌriːdəˈrekt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使改变方向，重新引导</td><td>redirect the funds<br>转拨资金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city redirected traffic away from the flooded underpass.  该市把车流从被淹的地下通道改道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swerve</td><td>/swɜːrv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>突然改变方向</td><td>swerve to avoid<br>急转以避开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The driver swerved sharply to avoid a dog crossing the road.  司机猛地转向，避开横穿马路的狗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swing</td><td>/ˈswɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>转向，摆动；秋千</td><td>swing sharply to the left<br>猛地转向左侧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bus swung left to avoid the fallen branch.  公交车向左急转以避开落下的树枝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wheel</td><td>/ˈwiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>突然转向；轮子</td><td>wheel around suddenly<br>突然转身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wheeled around when he heard footsteps behind him.  他听到身后的脚步声便猛地转身。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straighten</td><td>/ˈstreɪtən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使变直，弄整齐</td><td>straighten a wire<br>把铁丝弄直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He straightened the bent nail carefully with a small hammer.  他用小锤子小心地把弯钉子弄直。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vehement</b></td><td>/ˈviːəmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（情感）强烈的，热情的</td><td>vehement opposition<br>强烈的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a vehement protest against the new tax.  她对新税提出了强烈的抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ardent</td><td>/ˈɑːrdənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，热切的</td><td>an ardent supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an ardent fan of the team for fifty years.  他五十年来一直是这支球队的狂热球迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demonstrative</td><td>/dɪˈmɑːnstrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情外露的</td><td>a demonstrative greeting<br>热情外露的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her demonstrative hugs surprised colleagues used to formal handshakes.  她热情的拥抱让习惯正式握手的同事很惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emotional</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情丰富的；动情的</td><td>an emotional farewell speech<br>动情的告别演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became emotional when describing his father's last letter.  描述父亲最后一封信时他动了感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fervid</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的</td><td>a fervid supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fervid speech moved even the quietest people in the room.  他热情洋溢的演讲连房间里最安静的人都被感动了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impassioned</td><td>/ɪmˈpæʃənd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨激昂的，充满激情的</td><td>an impassioned speech<br>慷慨激昂的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impassioned defense of public libraries.  她慷慨激昂地为公共图书馆辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intense</td><td>/ɪnˈtens/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强烈的，极度的</td><td>intense heat<br>酷热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The intense heat inside the foundry forced the workers to rest hourly.  铸造厂内的酷热迫使工人每小时休息一次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passionate</td><td>/ˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满激情的</td><td>a passionate defense of art<br>对艺术的热情辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is passionate about protecting the city's old neighborhoods.  她对保护城市的老街区充满热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perfervid</del></td><td>/pərˈfɜrvɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极热情的，狂热的</td><td>perfervid enthusiasm<br>狂热的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His perfervid loyalty to the team bordered on obsession.  他对球队狂热的忠诚近乎痴迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torrid</td><td>/ˈtɔːrɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的，情感深厚的</td><td>the torrid zone<br>热带地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The torrid afternoon heat kept everyone indoors until the sun finally set.  酷热的午后让所有人待在室内，直到太阳终于落下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cold</td><td>/ˈkoʊld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>寒冷的；冷淡的</td><td>a cold morning<br>寒冷的早晨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The water was much too cold for anyone to swim.  水太冷了，没人能游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cool</td><td>/ˈkuːl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凉爽的；冷淡的</td><td>a cool reception<br>冷淡的接待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His proposal met a cool response from the committee.  他的提案得到委员会冷淡的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dispassionate</td><td>/dɪsˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静客观的，不带感情的</td><td>a dispassionate analysis<br>冷静客观的分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge gave a dispassionate summary of both arguments.  法官冷静客观地总结了双方的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 emotionless</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无感情的，冷漠的</td><td>an emotionless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He read the verdict in a flat, emotionless tone.  他用平板毫无感情的语调宣读了判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impassive</td><td>/ɪmˈpæsɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无感情的</td><td>remain impassive under pressure<br>压力下仍无动于衷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His face stayed impassive as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，他的脸上始终毫无表情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unemotional</td><td>/ˌʌnɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不动感情的，冷静的</td><td>an unemotional account<br>不带感情的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unemotional summary of the disaster unsettled the listeners.  她不带感情的灾难总结让听众不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>venal</b></td><td>/ˈviːnl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪污受贿的</td><td>a venal official<br>贪污受贿的官员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A venal official can be bought with a surprisingly modest bribe.  一个贪污受贿的官员用很少的钱就能买通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bribable</del></td><td>/ˈbraɪbəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可行贿的，会受贿的</td><td>a bribable inspector<br>会受贿的检查员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Smugglers relied on a few bribable guards at the border.  走私者靠边境上几个会受贿的守卫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corruptible</td><td>/kəˈrʌptəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易腐化的，可受贿的</td><td>corruptible officials<br>易被腐化的官员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Any system run by corruptible clerks will fail eventually.  由易腐化的职员运行的制度终将失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dirty</td><td>/ˈdɜːrti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>肮脏的；卑鄙的</td><td>dirty windows<br>脏污的窗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children came home with dirty hands and happy faces.  孩子们回家时手很脏，脸上却很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 purchasable</td><td>/ˈpɜrtʃəsəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用钱买通的</td><td>a purchasable verdict<br>可以买到的判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that court every verdict seemed purchasable.  在那个法庭上似乎每份判决都能买到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incorruptible</td><td>/ˌɪnkəˈrʌptəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>廉洁的，不可贿赂的</td><td>an incorruptible judge<br>廉洁的法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city needed an incorruptible auditor more than new laws.  这座城市比起新法更需要一位廉洁的审计员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vendor</b></td><td>/ˈvendər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>小贩，商贩</td><td>a street vendor<br>街头小贩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A street vendor sold hot noodles outside the station every evening.  每天傍晚都有个街头小贩在车站外卖热面条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broker</td><td>/ˈbroʊkər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>经纪人；中间人</td><td>an insurance broker<br>保险经纪人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neutral broker helped the two sides reach an agreement.  一位中立的中间人帮助双方达成协议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dealer</td><td>/ˈdiːlər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>经销商，商人</td><td>a car dealer<br>汽车经销商</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dealer offered a discount if we paid in cash.  经销商说现金付款可以打折。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 merchandiser</td><td>/ˈmɜːrtʃənˌdaɪzər/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>商品销售商</td><td>a clothing merchandiser<br>服装销售商</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The merchandiser rearranged the shelves before the sale began.  促销开始前销售商重新摆放了货架。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retailer</td><td>/ˈriːˌteɪlər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>零售商</td><td>a large retailer<br>大型零售商</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retailer stopped stocking the brand after months of complaints.  投诉数月后零售商停售了这个品牌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seller</td><td>/ˈselər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>卖主，销售者</td><td>the seller of the house<br>房屋的卖主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The seller accepted our offer on the very first day.  卖主第一天就接受了我们的报价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trader</td><td>/ˈtreɪdər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>商人，贸易商</td><td>a market trader<br>集市商贩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traders shouted prices across the crowded fish market.  商贩们在拥挤的鱼市上高声叫价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 buyer</td><td>/ˈbaɪər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>买主，购买者</td><td>find a buyer<br>找到买主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They finally found a buyer for the empty warehouse.  他们终于为那座空仓库找到了买主。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consumer</td><td>/kənˈsuːmər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>消费者</td><td>consumer demand<br>消费需求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Consumers switched to cheaper brands as prices climbed.  随着物价上涨，消费者转向更便宜的品牌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 purchaser</td><td>/ˈpɜːrtʃəsər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>购买者，买方</td><td>the original purchaser<br>最初的购买者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The warranty covers only the first purchaser of the device.  保修只覆盖该设备的首位购买者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>veneer</b></td><td>/vəˈnɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>虚饰，假象；薄薄的表层</td><td>a veneer of politeness<br>一层礼貌的虚饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His charm was only a veneer over real cruelty.  他的魅力只是掩盖真正残忍的一层虚饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blanch</td><td>/ˈblæntʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使变白，漂白；发白</td><td>blanch the almonds<br>把杏仁焯白</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blanched visibly when the bill was handed over.  账单递来时他明显脸色发白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 extenuate</del></td><td>/ɪkˈstenjueɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻罪过</td><td>extenuate the offense<br>减轻罪责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His youth did not extenuate so serious an offense.  他年纪轻并不能减轻如此严重的罪责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloss</td><td>/ɡlɑːs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>简短解释；敷衍潦草做事（甚至忽略）</td><td>gloss over the problem<br>敷衍带过问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor added a short gloss explaining the unfamiliar legal term.  编者加了一条简短的解释，说明这个陌生的法律术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 palliate</del></td><td>/ˈpælieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，缓和；掩饰</td><td>palliate the symptoms<br>缓解症状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new drug can only palliate the pain, not cure the disease.  这种新药只能减轻疼痛，无法治愈疾病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sugarcoat</td><td>/ˈʃʊɡərkoʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>粉饰，美化</td><td>sugarcoat bad news<br>粉饰坏消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor refused to sugarcoat the test results.  医生不愿粉饰检查结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 varnish</td><td>/ˈvɑːrnɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>粉饰（令人不悦的东西）</td><td>varnish the truth<br>粉饰真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The official tried to varnish the truth about the failed project.  那位官员试图粉饰这个失败项目的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bare</td><td>/ˈber/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>揭露，袒露；赤裸的</td><td>bare the truth<br>揭露真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the interview he bared his family's darkest secrets.  在采访中他揭露了家族最黑暗的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disclose</td><td>/dɪsˈkloʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，公开，透露</td><td>disclose the details<br>公开细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to disclose the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝公开和解协议的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discover</td><td>/dɪˈskʌvər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>发现；揭露</td><td>discover a hidden flaw<br>发现隐藏缺陷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Auditors discovered that the accounts had been altered.  审计员发现账目被改动过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 divulge</td><td>/daɪˈvʌldʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>泄漏（秘密）</td><td>divulge a secret<br>泄露秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No employee may divulge a secret learned during the merger negotiations.  任何员工都不得泄露在并购谈判中得知的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expose</td><td>/ɪkˈspoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露；使暴露</td><td>expose the fraud<br>揭露欺诈行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two journalists exposed the fraud after months of quiet investigation.  两名记者经过数月暗中调查揭露了这起欺诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reveal</td><td>/rɪˈviːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭示，透露</td><td>reveal the details<br>透露细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to reveal the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝透露和解的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncloak</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkloʊk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，揭开真相</td><td>uncloak a conspiracy<br>揭露阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist worked for months to uncloak the corruption scheme.  这位记者花了数月揭露这个腐败阴谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncover</td><td>/ənˈkʌvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭开，发现，揭露</td><td>uncover the evidence<br>发现证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors uncovered evidence of fraud in the accounting records.  检查人员在账目中发现了欺诈的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unmask</td><td>/ʌnˈmæsk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，揭穿真面目</td><td>unmask the impostor<br>揭穿那个冒充者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist unmasked the company's secret polluting practices.  记者揭露了这家公司秘密排污的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unveil</td><td>/ənˈveɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭幕，公布，揭示</td><td>unveil a new model<br>发布新款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company will unveil a new model at the spring show.  公司将在春季展会上发布新款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>venerate</b></td><td>/ˈvenəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>尊敬</td><td>venerate their ancestors<br>崇敬他们的祖先</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers still venerate their ancestors with offerings twice each year.  村民每年两次以供品崇敬他们的祖先。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adore</td><td>/əˈdɔːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>热爱，极喜爱；崇拜</td><td>absolutely adore someone<br>非常喜爱某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children adore their new puppy.  孩子们非常喜爱他们的新小狗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deify</td><td>/ˈdeɪɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>神化，奉若神明</td><td>deify a leader<br>神化领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime tried to deify its founder in every textbook.  该政权试图在每本教科书里神化其创始人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 esteem</td><td>/ɪˈstiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>尊重，敬重</td><td>hold in high esteem<br>十分敬重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her colleagues esteem her for her honesty and courage.  同事们因她的诚实与勇气而敬重她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regard</td><td>/rɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>关注，重视；看待</td><td>have regard for others<br>顾及他人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drives fast with little regard for other people.  他开车很快，几乎不顾及他人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 respect</td><td>/rɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尊敬，敬重；方面</td><td>show respect for<br>对……表示尊敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students should learn to respect opinions that differ from their own.  学生应学会尊重与自己不同的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revere</td><td>/rɪˈvɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（尤指对神的）尊崇，尊敬</td><td>revered as a hero<br>被尊崇为英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students in the village still revere the teacher who founded their school.  村里的学生仍然尊崇创办他们学校的那位老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 despise</td><td>/dɪˈspaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鄙视，极其不喜欢</td><td>despise a coward<br>鄙视懦夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She despised him for abandoning his closest friends.  她鄙视他抛弃至交的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disdain</td><td>/dɪsˈdeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>轻视，鄙视</td><td>disdain to answer<br>不屑于回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She disdained the flattery of her ambitious colleagues.  她鄙视那些野心勃勃的同事的奉承。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flout</td><td>/flaʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嘲弄性不理会，蔑视</td><td>flout the rules<br>蔑视规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drivers who flout the rules face heavy fines this year.  今年蔑视规则的司机将面临重罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scorn</td><td>/skɔːrn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>轻蔑，鄙视，不屑</td><td>scorn an offer<br>不屑接受提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He scorned the idea of asking his parents for money.  他不屑于向父母要钱这个想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>venial</b></td><td>/ˈviːniəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可宽恕的</td><td>a venial mistake<br>可宽恕的小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arriving five minutes late was a venial fault in her eyes.  在她看来，迟到五分钟是可以宽恕的小过失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 condonable</del></td><td>/kənˈdoʊnəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可宽恕的，可原谅的</td><td>hardly condonable<br>很难宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lateness is condonable, but lying about it is not.  迟到可以宽恕，但为此说谎不行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excusable</td><td>/ɪkˈskjuːzəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可原谅的</td><td>an excusable error<br>可以原谅的失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A slip on the first day is entirely excusable.  第一天出点差错完全可以原谅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forgivable</td><td>/fərˈɡɪvəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可宽恕的</td><td>a forgivable lapse<br>可宽恕的失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Forgetting one name at such a party is forgivable.  在这种聚会上忘记一个名字是可以宽恕的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pardonable</del></td><td>/ˈpɑrdənəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可饶恕的，情有可原的</td><td>pardonable pride<br>情有可原的自豪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She showed a pardonable pride in her daughter's work.  她对女儿的作品流露出情有可原的自豪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 remissible</del></td><td>/rɪˈmɪsəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可免除的，可宽赦的</td><td>a remissible penalty<br>可免除的处罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fine was remissible if he attended the safety course.  只要他上安全课，罚款便可免除。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 remittable</del></td><td>/rɪˈmɪtəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可减免的，可赦免的</td><td>a remittable debt<br>可减免的债务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only part of the interest was remittable under the new rule.  按新规定只有部分利息可以减免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heinous</td><td>/ˈheɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，十恶不赦的</td><td>a heinous act<br>令人憎恶的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the crime as heinous and utterly without excuse.  法庭称这一罪行十恶不赦，毫无借口可言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indefensible</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈfensəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>站不住脚的，无法辩解的</td><td>an indefensible decision<br>无法辩解的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the safety budget was an indefensible decision.  削减安全预算是个无法辩解的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inexcusable</td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈskjuːzəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可原谅的</td><td>inexcusable rudeness<br>不可原谅的无礼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Leaving the child alone in the car was inexcusable.  把孩子单独留在车里不可原谅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mortal</td><td>/ˈmɔːrtəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>终将死亡的，凡人的；致命的</td><td>mortal beings<br>凡人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play reminds us that even great kings are mortal men.  这部剧提醒我们连伟大的国王也是凡人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unforgivable</td><td>/ˌʌnfərˈɡɪvəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可宽恕的</td><td>an unforgivable betrayal<br>不可宽恕的背叛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 To her, hiding the diagnosis was unforgivable.  对她来说，隐瞒诊断结果不可宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpardonable</td><td>/ʌnˈpɑrdənəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可饶恕的</td><td>an unpardonable insult<br>不可饶恕的侮辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His remark about her family was unpardonable.  他对她家人的那番话不可饶恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>venom</b></td><td>/ˈvenəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>毒液；恶意，恶毒</td><td>spit venom at critics<br>对批评者恶语相向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke with such venom that the whole room fell silent.  他说话时如此恶毒，整个房间都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despite</td><td>/dɪˈspaɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶意，轻蔑</td><td>act out of despite<br>出于恶意行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He damaged the painting out of pure despite for its owner.  他纯出恶意，损坏了那幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malevolence</td><td>/məˈlevələns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶意，恶毒</td><td>sheer malevolence<br>纯粹的恶意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was open malevolence in the anonymous letters.  那些匿名信里满是公然的恶意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maliciousness</td><td>/məˈlɪʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶毒，怀恨</td><td>unexpected maliciousness<br>意想不到的恶毒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The maliciousness of the rumor shocked even her critics.  谣言之恶毒连她的批评者都震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 malignance</del></td><td>/məˈlɪɡnəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶意，狠毒</td><td>hidden malignance<br>隐藏的狠毒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Behind the polite words lay real malignance.  礼貌措辞背后藏着真正的狠毒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 malignity</del></td><td>/məˈlɪɡnəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶毒，深仇</td><td>cold malignity<br>冷酷的恶毒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pursued the lawsuit with astonishing malignity.  他以惊人的恶毒不断打这场官司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meanness</td><td>/ˈmiːnnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>刻薄，卑劣</td><td>petty meanness<br>小气刻薄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The meanness of his joke silenced the whole table.  他玩笑的刻薄让整桌人沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nastiness</td><td>/ˈnæstinəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶意，下流</td><td>online nastiness<br>网络上的恶意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nastiness in the comments forced her offline for weeks.  评论中的恶意让她数周不敢上网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spite</td><td>/ˈspaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>恶意，怨恨；刁难</td><td>out of pure spite<br>纯粹出于恶意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He broke the toy out of spite after losing the game.  输了游戏后，他出于恶意弄坏了玩具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 viciousness</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəsnɪs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>凶狠，恶毒</td><td>the viciousness of the attack<br>袭击的凶狠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors were stunned by the viciousness of the quarrel.  邻居们对这场争吵的凶狠感到震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comity</td><td>/ˈkɑːmɪti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>友好，礼让；和睦</td><td>the comity of nations<br>国家间的礼让</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty was signed in a spirit of genuine comity.  条约是在真诚友好的气氛中签署的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 friendship</td><td>/ˈfrendʃɪp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>友谊，友好</td><td>a lasting friendship<br>持久的友谊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their friendship survived twenty years and three continents.  他们的友谊经历了二十年和三大洲的考验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 goodwill</td><td>/ˈɡʊdwɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>善意，友好</td><td>a gesture of goodwill<br>善意的表示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They returned the boat as a gesture of goodwill.  他们归还了那条船，以示善意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
